\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-1,1)
with
\varphi\equiv 1
on
[-1/2,1/2]
. Recall the definition (
3.35
) for
d_{j,k}
. Assume that

{\rm supp}\,h\subseteq S^{j}_{9\delta}.

Denote

\epsilon:=\nu/k,\,\,M:=\|h\|_{L^{2}(S^{j}_{9\delta})}+d_{j,k}(\Lambda)\|\partial_{y}h\|_{L^{2}(S^{j}_{9\delta})}.

(3.54)

There exists a decomposition for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

A_{\Theta}^{-1}h(y):=w_{1}(y,\lambda)+w_{2}(y,\lambda),

(3.55)

satisfying the property that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

|w_{1}(y,\lambda)|\lesssim\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\Big[d_{j,k}(\Lambda)|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\Big]^{-1}|y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda}|^{-1/2}M+\Big[\big(d_{j,k}(\Lambda)\big)^{1/2}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|\Big]^{-1}M,

(3.56)

and

\begin{split}&\bigg|w_{2}(y,\lambda)-\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\frac{h(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})\varphi\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)}{ib^{\prime}(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})(y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})+|\epsilon|^{1/3}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/3}}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{-1/2}\frac{|\epsilon|^{-1/3}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/6}}{1+(|\epsilon|^{1/3}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{-1/6})^{-2}|y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda}|^{2}}\big(d_{j,k}(\Lambda)\big)^{-1/2}M.\end{split}

(3.57)

(ii) Assume that
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\backslash\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
.
In this case there exist
y^{+}_{\lambda},y^{-}_{\lambda}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\backslash S_{j,\delta_{0}}
, arranged so that
y^{+}_{\lambda}
is in the positive direction, counter-clockwise, to
y^{-}_{\lambda}
, such that

\lambda=b(y^{+}_{\lambda})=b(y^{-}_{\lambda}).

(3.58)

Denote

\epsilon:=\nu/k,\,\,M:=\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}+|k|^{-1}\|\partial_{y}h\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

(3.59)

There exists a decomposition for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}

A_{\Theta}^{-1}h(y):=w_{1}(y,\lambda)+w_{2}(y,\lambda),

(3.60)

satisfying the property that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

|w_{1}(y,\lambda)|\lesssim\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}|k||y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda}|^{-1/2}M,

(3.61)

and for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&\bigg|w_{2}(y,\lambda)-\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\frac{h(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})\varphi(y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})}{ib^{\prime}(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})(y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})+|\epsilon|^{1/3}}\bigg|\lesssim\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\frac{|\epsilon|^{-1/3}}{1+|\epsilon|^{-2/3}|y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda}|^{2}}|k|^{1/2}M.\end{split}

(3.62)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\epsilon>0
. We focus on the proof of the case (i), with (ii) being similar and simpler. Define for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

w(y,\lambda):=A_{\Theta}^{-1}h(y).

(3.63)

It follows from Lemma
3.3
, the bound (
3.4
) and Sobolev inequalities that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with

\min\{|y-y_{\lambda}^{+}|,|y-y_{\lambda}^{-}|\}\gtrsim|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}

we have bound

|w(y,\lambda)|\lesssim|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{-1}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim\Big[\big(d_{j,k}(\Lambda)\big)^{1/2}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|\Big]^{-1}M.

(3.64)

Note that
w
satisfies for
\dagger\in\{+,-\}
,
|y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda}|\ll|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}
,

\begin{split}&\Big[\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}+ib^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger}-y)-\alpha\Big]w(y,\lambda)\\
&=h(y)+i\Big[b^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger}-y)+b(y)-b(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})\Big]w(y,\lambda)\\
&=h(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})+h(y)-h(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})+i\Big[b^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger}-y)+b(y)-b(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})\Big]w(y,\lambda).\end{split}

(3.65)

To extract the most singular part near
y=y^{\dagger}_{\lambda}
, we define for
y\in\mathbb{R}
,
W(y,\Lambda)
as the solution to

\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}-iy-\epsilon^{-1/3}|b^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})|^{-2/3}\alpha\Big]W(y,\Lambda)=1.

(3.66)

It follows from Proposition 3.1 in
[
41
]
that
W(y,\Lambda)
satisfies the following bounds for
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\Big|W(y,\Lambda)-\frac{1}{1-iy}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{1+y^{2}}.

(3.67)

The desired bounds then follow from Lemma
3.3
and the inequality (
3.67
) using equation (
3.65
), by taking

w_{2}(y,\lambda):=\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\epsilon^{-1/3}|b^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})|^{-2/3}h(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})W\Big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})\epsilon^{-1/3}|b^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})|^{1/3},\Lambda\Big)\varphi\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big).

(3.68)

∎

Remark 3.2
.

Denote for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},\dagger\in\{+,-\}
,

g(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}h(z)\,dz.

(3.69)

It follows from (
3.55
) and (
3.57
), using integration by parts and the identity for
\iota\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}

\frac{1}{y-y^{\dagger}+i\iota}=\partial_{y}\log\frac{y-y^{\dagger}+i\iota}{\delta},

(3.70)

that

\begin{split}&\left\|g\right\|_{L^{2}(S^{j}_{9\delta})}+d_{j,k}(\Lambda)\left\|\partial_{y}g\right\|_{L^{2}(S^{j}_{9\delta})}\lesssim\frac{d_{j,k}^{1/2}(\Lambda)}{\delta^{1/2}}M,\end{split}

(3.71)

and for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\backslash S^{j}_{9\delta}
,

\begin{split}&|g(y)|+d_{j,k}(\Lambda)|\partial_{y}g(y)|\lesssim d_{j,k}^{-1/2}\delta^{-1}\Big[\delta^{-1}\int_{|z-y_{j\ast}|<10\delta}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)\,dz\Big]\,M.\end{split}

(3.72)

4.
The limiting absorption principle and main bounds on the spectral density function

In this section we state the limiting absorption principle, and derive bounds on the spectral density functions using the limiting absorption principle and the equations (
1.18
).

4.1.
The limiting absorption principle for the Orr-Sommerfeld equation

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty),\nu\in(0,1/8),\lambda\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\geq-\sigma_{0}|\nu/k|^{1/2}
with
\sigma_{0}\in(0,1/2)
sufficiently small from Proposition
3.1
, denoting
\Theta:=(\lambda,\alpha,\nu,k,j)
,
we define the operator
T_{\Theta}:H^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to H^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
as follows.

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\big(\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big)
or
\alpha\geq\delta_{0}
, we define for any
h\in H^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

T_{\Theta}h(y):=\Delta_{k}^{-1}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(ib^{\prime\prime}h\big)(y),

(4.1)

where we used the notation
\Delta_{k}:=\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2}
.

If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
and
-\sigma_{0}|\nu/k|^{1/2}\leq\alpha\leq\delta_{0}
, for some
j\in\{1,2\}
, then we define for any
h\in H^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}T_{\Theta}h(y):=-\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)&\Big\{A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(ib^{\prime\prime}h\big)(z)\\
&\quad+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(z)}{b(z)-\lambda-i\alpha}\Big[\varphi_{0}\big(\frac{z-y_{j\ast}}{\delta_{0}}\big)-\varphi_{0}\big(\frac{z-y_{j\ast}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\big)\Big]h(z)\Big\}dz.\end{split}

(4.2)

The main technical tool we need is the following limiting absorption principle.

Proposition 4.1
.

Fix
\gamma\in[7/4,2)
. Under the assumptions in Proposition
1.1
, there exist
\nu_{0},\kappa,\sigma_{\sharp}\in(0,1/10)
sufficiently small depending on
\gamma
, such that the following statement holds. Assume that

(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in\big\{(0,-\gamma),(1,-\gamma+1),(1-2(2-\gamma),-\gamma+2)\big\}.

(4.3)

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty),\nu\in(0,\nu_{0}),\alpha\geq-\sigma_{\sharp}|\nu/k|^{1/2}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the following bounds.

(i) If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
for some
j\in\{1,2\}
, then for any
h\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
as in (
4.3
),

\big\|h+T_{\Theta}h\big\|_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\geq\kappa\|h\|_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})};

(4.4)

(ii) If
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\big(\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big)
, then

\big\|h+T_{\Theta}h\big\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\geq\kappa\|h\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

(4.5)

Remark 4.1
.

We briefly explain the design of the exponents (
4.3
) and the weighted norm (
1.41
), which will be used in section
4.2
to control the spectral density functions and their derivatives in
\lambda
up to two derivatives.

The weights used in Proposition
4.1
are motivated by the decay of the modified Green’s functions near the critical points
y_{1\ast},y_{2\ast}
. Assuming
k=1
and
\min\{|z-y_{1\ast}|,|z-y_{2\ast}|\}\gtrsim 1
for simplicity, the modified Green’s function
\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)\sim(|y-y_{j\ast}|+\delta(\Lambda))^{2}

for
y
close to
y_{j\ast}
. This asymptotic matches the weights used in (
1.41
) for
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=(0,-\gamma)
. The use of
L^{2}
and
L^{\infty}
based spaces adapts to the singularity at
\lambda=b(y)
that appears in (
4.2
).

For
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=(0,-\gamma)
and
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=(1,-\gamma+1)
, the corresponding norms reflect the heuristic that taking one derivative in
\lambda
costs
\varrho_{j}^{-1}\varrho_{j,k}^{-1}
(in contrast to taking one derivative in
y
which costs only
\varrho_{j,k}^{-1}
). The extra adjustment for
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=(1-2(2-\gamma),-\gamma+2)
, where the weight
\varrho^{1-2(2-\gamma)}_{j}\varrho_{j,k}^{-\gamma+2}
was used in favor of the seemingly more natural weight
\varrho^{2}_{j}\varrho_{j,k}^{-\gamma+2}
, is due to the requirement that the weights need to vary more slowly than the modified Green’s function to avoid nonlocal effects. In applications below (see (
4.11
)), we need to incorporate an additional factor
\delta(\Lambda)^{1+2(2-\gamma)}
to match the two derivatives taken.

4.2.
Main bounds on the spectral density function

Assume that
j\in\{1,2\},k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
|\alpha|\leq\sigma_{\sharp}|\nu/k|^{1/2}
and
\gamma\in[7/4,2)
.

We define the “modified spectral density function”
\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\lambda)
for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
as

\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda):=\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\frac{\omega_{0k}(y)}{ib^{\prime\prime}(y)}.

(4.6)

Then
\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\lambda)
satisfies the equation for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}+i(\lambda-b(y))-\alpha\Big]\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)=f_{0k}(y,\Lambda),&\\
(-k^{2}+\partial_{y}^{2})\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)=\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),&\end{array}\right.

(4.7)

where

\begin{split}f_{0k}(y,\Lambda):=&\,\,\omega_{0k}(y)-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\omega_{0k}(y)\\
&-\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}+i(\lambda-b(y))-\alpha\Big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\Big\{\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\frac{\omega_{0k}(y)}{ib^{\prime\prime}(y)}\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.8)

The proof of our main theorems is based on the following proposition on the regularity of the spectral density function.

Proposition 4.2
.

Fix
\gamma\in[15/8,2)
and
\nu_{0},\sigma_{\sharp}\in(0,1)
from Proposition
4.1
. Assume that
\nu\in(0,\nu_{0})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
\epsilon:=\nu/k\in(0,1/8)
,
|\alpha|\leq\sigma_{\sharp}|\nu/k|^{1/2}
. Let
\omega_{0k},\omega_{k,\nu},\psi_{k,\nu}
be defined as in Proposition
1.1
, and let
\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}
be as in (
4.7
)-(
4.8
).

Define the “good derivative”
D_{\lambda}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R},y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
as follows.
If
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\min\{|\lambda-b(y_{1\ast})|,|\lambda-b(y_{2\ast})|\}\geq 10|\nu/k|^{1/2}
, then

D_{\lambda}:=\partial_{\lambda}+\Big[1-\varphi_{0}\big(\frac{y-y_{1\ast}}{\delta_{2}(\lambda)}\big)\Big]\Big[1-\varphi_{0}\big(\frac{y-y_{2\ast}}{\delta_{2}(\lambda)}\big)\Big]\frac{1}{b^{\prime}(y)}\partial_{y}.

(4.9)

If
\lambda\not\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
or
\min\{|\lambda-b(y_{1\ast})|,|\lambda-b(y_{2\ast})|\}<10|\nu/k|^{1/2}
, denote
D_{\lambda}:=\partial_{\lambda}
.

Then we have the following bounds.

(i) For
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\big(\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big),\sigma\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\big\|D_{\lambda}^{\sigma}\,\psi_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim|k|^{-5/2+\sigma}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda\rangle^{\sigma+1}}\big\|\omega_{0k}\big\|_{H^{3}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})};

(4.10)

(ii) For
j\in\{1,2\},\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
,

\begin{split}&\big\|\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big\|_{X^{0,-\gamma}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}\big\|\omega_{0k}\big\|_{H^{3}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})},\\
&\big\|D_{\lambda}\,\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big\|_{X^{1,-\gamma+1}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}\big\|\omega_{0k}\big\|_{H^{3}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})},\\
&\big\|D_{\lambda}^{2}\,\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big\|_{X^{1-2(2-\gamma),-\gamma+2}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}(\delta(\Lambda))^{-1-2(2-\gamma)}\big\|\omega_{0k}\big\|_{H^{3}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.\end{split}

(4.11)

As a corollary of Lemma
3.4
and Proposition
4.2
, we also have the following refined description of the singularities of
\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)
when
y
approaches the points
\{y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}:b(y)=\lambda\}
.

Corollary 4.1
.

There exist
\nu_{0},\sigma_{\sharp}>0
sufficiently small, such that for
0<\nu<\nu_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
,
|\alpha|\leq\sigma_{\sharp}|\nu/k|^{1/2}
, the following statement holds. Denote

M_{k}:=\|\omega_{0k}\|_{H^{3}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

(4.12)

(i) Assume that
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\cap b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|\gg|\nu/k|^{1/2}
.
Then we have the bounds for
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll|b(y)-b(y_{j\ast})|
,

|\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim\big(|y-y_{j\ast}|\wedge\frac{1}{|k|}\big)^{\gamma-2}|y-y_{j\ast}|^{-1}\bigg(1+\bigg|\log{\bigg|\frac{\lambda-b(y)}{b(y)-b(y_{j\ast})}\bigg|}\bigg|\bigg)|k|^{-1/2}M_{k},

(4.13)

and

\begin{split}&\bigg|\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+\frac{ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)}{i(\lambda-b(y))+|\nu/k|^{1/3}|y-y_{j\ast}|^{2/3}}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma-1/2}(y,\Lambda)|y-y_{j\ast}|^{-1/2}\frac{|\nu/k|^{-1/3}|y-y_{j\ast}|^{1/3}}{1+(|\nu/k|^{1/3}|y-y_{j\ast}|^{2/3})^{-2}|\lambda-b(y)|^{2}}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.\end{split}

(4.14)

(ii) If
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\backslash\big(\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big)
, we have the bounds for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll 1
,

|\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim\big(1+|\log{|\lambda-b(y)|}|\big)|k|^{-1/2}M_{k},

(4.15)

and

\begin{split}&\bigg|\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-\frac{\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)}{i(\lambda-b(y))+|\nu/k|^{1/3}}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\nu/k|^{-1/3}}{1+|\nu/k|^{-2/3}|\lambda-b(y)|^{2}}|k|^{-5/2}M_{k}.\end{split}

(4.16)

The proof of Corollary
4.1
on the refined properties of the functions
\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}
,
\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}
and
\omega_{k,\nu}^{\ast}
follows from equations (
4.7
), (
4.22
), and (
4.55
) using Proposition
4.2
and Lemmas
3.3
and
2.1
. These refined bounds are useful below in the proof of the main theorem.
The rest of the section is devoted to the proofs of Proposition
4.2
. We organize the proofs into different sections as the details of the argument are somewhat lengthy.

4.3.
Proof for statement (i) of Proposition
4.2

We divide the proof into the cases
\sigma\in\{0,1,2\}
, organized in subsections.

4.3.1.
The case
\sigma=0
for statement (i) of Proposition
4.2

To establish (
4.2
) for
\sigma=0
, by the limiting absorption principle (
4.5
), it suffices to show that

\big\|(k^{2}-\partial_{y}^{2})^{-1}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\omega_{0k}\big\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim|k|^{-5/2}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-1}M_{k}.

(4.17)

If
\lambda\not\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, the desired bounds (
4.17
) follow from Lemma
3.3
and standard elliptic estimates, since in this case
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
does not involve any singularities.

We now consider the case
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.
We note first the inequality that

\Big\|\omega_{0k}\Big\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim|k|^{-2}M_{k}.

(4.18)

To prove (
4.17
), we use (
4.18
), Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
, by considering the regions (I)
|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim 1
and (II)
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll 1
respectively. In the region (I), the desired bound (
4.17
) follows from (
4.18
) and Lemma
3.3
. In region (II), we use Lemma
3.4
. After peeling off the easier bounds for
\dagger\in\{+,-\}
(using the notation as in Lemma
3.4
)

\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}G_{k}(y,z)\frac{|\nu/k|^{-1/3}}{1+|\nu/k|^{-2/3}|z-y_{\lambda}^{\dagger}|^{2}}dz\bigg\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2},

(4.19)

it suffices to notice the inequality that for
\dagger\in\{+,-\}
(with the notation that
\epsilon^{\prime}:=|\nu/k|^{1/3}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/3}/b^{\prime}(y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})
),

\bigg\||k|^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||y-z|}\frac{\varphi(z-y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})}{i(z-y_{\lambda}^{\dagger})+\epsilon^{\prime}}dz\bigg\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}.

(4.20)

This completes the proof of (
4.10
) for
\sigma=0
.

4.3.2.
The case
\sigma=1
for statement (i) of Proposition
4.2

We now turn to the case
\sigma=1
.
Set for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

a(y,\lambda):=\Big[1-\varphi_{0}\big(\frac{y-y_{1\ast}}{\delta_{2}(\lambda)}\big)\Big]\Big[1-\varphi_{0}\big(\frac{y-y_{2\ast}}{\delta_{2}(\lambda)}\big)\Big]\frac{1}{b^{\prime}(y)}.

(4.21)

Taking the derivative
D_{\lambda}
, by a straightforward but somewhat lengthy calculation, we obtain that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(ib^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y)\\
&=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(g^{\ast}_{0k}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y)+\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda),\end{split}

(4.22)

where for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}g^{\ast}_{0k}(y,\Lambda):=&\,\,D_{\lambda}\omega_{0k}(y)+\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-ib^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&+i\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.23)

The calculations to get (
4.22
)-(
4.23
) are similar to those in subsection
4.4.2
which are slightly more complicated. For the sake of conciseness, we omit the repetitive details here. By Proposition
4.1
, it suffices to show for

\begin{split}h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{&D_{\lambda}\omega_{0k},\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\},\end{split}

(4.24)

and

\begin{split}h\in\Big\{\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu},\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big\},\end{split}

(4.25)

the following bounds hold

\big\|(k^{2}-\partial_{y}^{2})^{-1}h\big\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim\langle\lambda\rangle^{-2}|k|^{-3/2}M_{k}.

(4.26)

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\Big[b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cup\big(\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big)\Big]
, then the desired bounds (
4.26
) follow from (
4.10
) for the case
\sigma=0
easily since in this case
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
does not involve strong singularities. Below we assume that
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\backslash\big(\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big)
. The desired bounds for

\begin{split}h\in\Big\{&A_{\Theta}^{-1}D_{\lambda}\omega_{0k},A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(b^{\prime\prime\prime}a(y,\lambda)\psi_{k,\nu}),\\
&A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big[(b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1)\omega_{k,\nu}\big],\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big\},\end{split}

(4.27)

follow from the case
\sigma=0
, using the inequalities (
4.19
)-(
4.20
) and Lemma
3.4
. We note that
b^{\prime}a-1\equiv 0
in a neighborhood of
\{y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}:b(y)-\lambda=0\}
. Hence
(b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1)\omega_{k,\nu}
is not singular.

To handle the case
h=\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}
, we use the identity for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)=k^{2}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).

(4.28)

The desired bounds (
4.26
) for
h=\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}
then follow from analogous argument as in the proof of (
4.17
). It remains to prove (
4.26
) for

\begin{split}h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\},\end{split}

(4.29)

We consider only the harder case when
h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}\Big]
which is more singular.

It follows from equation (
1.18
) that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}+i(\lambda-b(y))-\alpha\Big]\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)=\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).

(4.30)

Thus for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}+i(\lambda-b(y))-\iota\alpha\Big]\partial_{y}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&=\partial_{y}\big[\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\big]+ib^{\prime}(y)\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.31)

Using the bounds

\big\|\partial_{y}\big[\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\big]\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim|k|^{-2}M_{k},

(4.32)

we obtain from Lemma
3.3
that

\begin{split}&\Big\|\frac{\nu}{k}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{\partial_{y}a\,\partial_{y}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\partial_{y}\big[\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\big]\Big\}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|(i(\lambda-b(y))+|\nu/k|^{1/3})^{-2}\frac{\nu}{k}L^{-1}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}\big[\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\\
&\lesssim|k|^{-2}M_{k},\end{split}

(4.33)

and for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&\Big|\frac{\nu}{k}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{\partial_{y}a\,(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(b^{\prime}\omega_{k,\nu}\big)(y,\Lambda)\Big\}\Big|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{(|\lambda-b(y)|+|\nu/k|^{1/3})^{3}}|\nu/k|\frac{|k|^{-2}M_{k}}{|\nu/k|^{1/2}(|\nu/k|^{1/3}+|\lambda-b(y)|)^{-1/2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\nu/k|^{-1/3}}{(1+|\nu/k|^{-1/3}|\lambda-b(y)|)^{5/2}}|k|^{-2}M_{k}.\end{split}

(4.34)

The desired bounds (
4.26
) for
h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}\Big]
follow from (
4.31
), and (
4.33
)-(
4.34
). This completes the proof of (
4.26
) and therefore also the bound (
4.11
) for
\sigma=1
.

4.3.3.
The case
\sigma=2
for statement (i) of Proposition
4.2

Similar calculations to (
4.68
)-(
4.71
) below (which we again omit to avoid repetitive details) show that

\begin{split}&(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(ib^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y)\\
&=A_{\Theta}^{-1}g_{0k}^{\ast\ast}(y,\Lambda)+\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda),\end{split}

(4.35)

where we have defined

\begin{split}g_{0k}^{\ast\ast}(y,\Lambda):=&D_{\lambda}\Big\{g_{0k}^{\ast}+A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\Big\}\\
&+\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}\Big]D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&+i\Big[a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime}(y)-1\Big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-ia(y,\lambda)b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.36)

As in the case of
\sigma=1
, it suffices to show that for

\begin{split}&h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{D_{\lambda}g_{0k}^{\ast},D_{\lambda}A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big],\\
&\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&\Big[a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime}(y)-1\Big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\},\end{split}

(4.37)

and for

h\in\Big\{\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu},\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big\},

(4.38)

we have the bound

\big\|(k^{2}-\partial_{y}^{2})^{-1}h\big\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim\langle\lambda\rangle^{-3}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.

(4.39)

To prove the bounds (
4.39
) we shall use the following commutator relations for any
q(x,\lambda)
with sufficient regularity,

\begin{split}\big[A_{\Theta},D_{\lambda}\big]&=i\big(a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime}(y)-1\big)+\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2},\\
\big[D_{\lambda},q(x,\lambda)\big]&=D_{\lambda}q(x,\lambda),\,\,\big[D_{\lambda},\partial_{y}\big]=-\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}.\end{split}

(4.40)

We observe from (
4.40
) that the commutator terms behave better than the uncommutated terms, by either gaining a factor of
\nu/k
or one derivative.

Using equation (
4.22
), the commutator relations (
4.40
) and the identity

\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)=\big(\partial^{2}_{y}-k^{2}\big)D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+k^{2}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),

(4.41)

the desired bounds for

h\in\Big\{\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu},\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big\}

(4.42)

and

\begin{split}&h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{D_{\lambda}A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}\Big],\\
&\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&\Big[a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime}(y)-1\Big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\},\end{split}

(4.43)

follow from the bounds (
4.10
) for
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
and the arguments as in proof of the cases
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
, after peeling off the easier commutator terms.

It remains to prove (
4.39
) for
h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}D_{\lambda}g_{0k}^{\ast}
. Using the definition (
4.23
), it suffices to prove (
4.39
) for

\begin{split}h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}D_{\lambda}\Big\{&\,D_{\lambda}\omega_{0k}(y),\,\,\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\,\,b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.44)

Following similar arguments as in the proof for
h
as in (
4.42
)-(
4.43
) and considering only the more singular terms, it suffices to prove (
4.39
) for

\begin{split}h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}D_{\lambda}\Big\{&\,\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.45)

After peeling off the easier commutator terms using (
4.40
), we reduce to prove (
4.39
) for

\begin{split}h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{&\,\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\\
&\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.46)

The desired bounds (
4.39
) for
h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big\{\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}\big\}(y,\Lambda)
follows from Lemma
3.4
noting that
b^{\prime}a-1
vanishes in a neighborhood of
\{y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\,b(y)=\lambda\}
. The proof of (
4.39
) for
A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big\{\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}\big\}(y,\Lambda)
is similar to (
4.30
)-(
4.34
).

4.4.
Proof of statement (ii) in Proposition
4.2

We choose
\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in(1,2)
with
\gamma<\gamma_{2}<\gamma_{1}<\gamma_{0}<2
, and
\nu_{0}
sufficiently small such that Proposition
4.2
holds for
\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
.

Fix
j\in\{1,2\}
. We provide the detailed proof only for
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
with
|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|\gtrsim|\nu/k|^{1/2}
. The case when
|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|\ll|\nu/k|^{1/2}
or
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\backslash b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
is easier since the singular behavior of
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
for
|y-y_{j\ast}|\lesssim|\nu/k|^{1/4}
is compensated by the decay property of the modified Green’s function and we can use Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
to get effective bounds.

The following weighted bounds are very useful to treat various nonlocal contributions below.

Lemma 4.1
.

We have the following pointwise inequality for
\sigma_{1}\in(-1,1),\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-1,2)
, any
h\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)\varrho_{j}^{\sigma_{1}-1}\varrho_{j,k}^{\sigma_{2}-1}(z;\Lambda)h(z)\Big|dz\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\varrho_{j}^{\sigma_{1}-1}\varrho_{j,k}^{\sigma_{2}+1}(y;\Lambda)\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

(4.47)

The proof follows from direct calculations using the bounds on the modified Green’s function in Lemma
2.1
.

The inequality (
4.47
) is useful when
|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|
so that
A^{-1}_{\Theta}
is not singular. To handle the case when
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|
, in view of Lemma
3.4
and adopting the notation there, we use the following bounds. Denote
\epsilon:=\nu/k
and define for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\mathcal{I}_{k,\nu}(y,\lambda):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\frac{\varphi_{0}\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)}{ib^{\prime}(y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})(y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})+|\epsilon|^{1/3}|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/3}}dz.

(4.48)

Then we have,

\begin{split}&d^{-1/2}_{j,k}\big(\delta(\Lambda)\big)^{-1}\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1\}}\big\|d^{\beta}_{j,k}(\Lambda)\partial^{\beta}_{y}\mathcal{I}_{k,\nu}(y,\lambda)\big\|_{L^{2}(S^{j}_{8\delta})}\\
&+d^{-2}_{j,k}(\Lambda)\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\varrho_{j,k}^{1+\beta}(y,\Lambda)\partial^{\beta}_{y}\mathcal{I}_{k,\nu}(y,\lambda)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\backslash S^{j}_{8\delta})}\lesssim|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{-1/2}.\end{split}

(4.49)

We prove the bounds for
D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\,\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast},\sigma\in\{0,1,2\}
, respectively, organized as subsections.

4.4.1.
Proof of the bounds for
D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\,\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast},\sigma=0
in statement (ii) in Proposition
4.2

Using the bounds (
4.4
) of the limiting absorption principle and the equation (
4.7
)-(
4.8
), it suffices to show that

\big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)A_{k,\nu}^{-1}(\Lambda)f_{0k}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big\|_{X^{0,-\gamma_{0}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.

(4.50)

In the above, with a slight abuse of notation, we denote for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty),\nu\in(0,1/18),\lambda\in\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
,
\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda):H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
as follows. For any
h\in H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
,

\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)h(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)h(z)dz.

(4.51)

Notice that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}A_{\Theta}^{-1}f_{0k}(\cdot,\Lambda)=&\,A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big[\omega_{0k}(y)-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\omega_{0k}(y)\Big]\\
&-(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\frac{\omega_{0k}(y)}{ib^{\prime\prime}(y)}\Big].\end{split}

(4.52)

The desired bounds (
4.50
) follow from Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
and the bounds on the modified Green’s function.

4.4.2.
Proof of the bounds for
D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\,\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast},\sigma=1
in statement (ii) in Proposition
4.2

We note that
\partial_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}
satisfies the equation

\begin{split}A_{\Theta}\partial_{\lambda}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\partial_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)&=-i\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&\quad+\partial_{\lambda}f_{0k}(y,\Lambda),\\
(-k^{2}+\partial_{y}^{2})\partial_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)&=\partial_{\lambda}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.53)

Direct computation shows that
a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}
satisfies the equation for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&A_{\Theta}(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\Big[a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big]+ib^{\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&=a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}f_{0k}(y,\Lambda)+A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\\
&\,+\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&\,-ib^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+ib^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.54)

It follows from equations (
4.53
) and (
4.54
) that
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(ib^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y)\\
&=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(f_{0k}^{\ast}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y)+\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda),\end{split}

(4.55)

where for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}f_{0k}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda):=&\,\,D_{\lambda}f_{0k}(y,\Lambda)+\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-ib^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&+i\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.56)

Using the bounds (
4.4
) of the limiting absorption principle and the equation (
4.55
)-(
4.56
), it suffices to show that

\big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)h\big\|_{X^{1,-\gamma+1}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}M_{k},

(4.57)

where

h\in\Big\{A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(f_{0k}^{\ast}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y),\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\Big\}.

(4.58)

To prove (
4.57
), we divide into the singular region for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|
and the non-singular region for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|
. More precisely, using the notations in Lemma
3.4
, it suffices to prove that for
h
as in (
4.58
),

\Big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)\Big[1-\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)\Big]h\Big\|_{X^{1,-\gamma_{1}+1}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}M_{k},

(4.59)

and for
\dagger\in\{+,-\}

\big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)\varphi_{0}\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)h\big\|_{X^{1,-\gamma_{1}+1}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.

(4.60)

To establish (
4.59
), we notice the bounds that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},\dagger\in\{+,-\}
and
h
as in (
4.58
),

\Big|\Big[1-\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\varphi_{0}\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)\Big]h(y)\Big|\lesssim\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma_{0}-2}\varrho_{j}^{-2}(y;\Lambda)\lesssim\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma_{1}-2}\varrho_{j}^{\gamma_{0}-\gamma_{1}-2}(y;\Lambda).

(4.61)

The desired bounds (
4.59
) then follow from the estimates (
4.51
).

To establish (
4.60
), we use similar arguments as in section
4.3.2
, since the weights are constant over the support of
\varphi\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)
and can be taken out of the estimates when appropriately accounted for. We consider the terms

h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{D_{\lambda}f_{0k},\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a\partial_{y}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a\partial_{y}^{2}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu},b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big\}

(4.62)

and

h\in\Big\{\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\}.

(4.63)

Using (
4.8
) and setting for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

p(y)=(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\omega_{0k}(y)\Big],

by direct calculations we obtain that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}D_{\lambda}f_{0k}=&a\partial_{y}\Big[\omega_{0k}(y)-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\omega_{0k}(y)\Big]+i(ab^{\prime}-1)p\\
&-A_{\Theta}\Big[a\,\partial_{y}p\Big]+\frac{\nu}{k}\Big[2\partial_{y}(\partial_{y}\partial_{y}p)-\partial_{y}^{2}a\partial_{y}p\Big],\end{split}

(4.64)

To prove the desired bound (
4.60
) for
h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}D_{\lambda}f_{0k}
, by (
4.64
), noting the support property of the first and second terms on the right hand side of (
4.64
) and using integration by parts in the third term after cancelling
A_{\Theta}
, it suffices to consider the term

h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\frac{\nu}{k}\Big[2\partial_{y}(\partial_{y}\partial_{y}p)-\partial_{y}^{2}a\partial_{y}p\Big],

(4.65)

which follows from Lemma
3.3
.

The desired bounds (
4.60
) for

h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a\partial_{y}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a\partial_{y}^{2}\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\big[b^{\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)-1\big]\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big\}

(4.66)

follow from Lemma
3.3
and equation (
4.7
) noting the support property of
b^{\prime}a-1
. The desired bounds for

h\in\Big\{b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\}

(4.67)

follow from equation (
4.7
) and Lemma (
3.4
), as in (
4.28
). We omit the lengthy but straightforward calculations and refer to (
4.29
)-(
4.34
) for details.

4.4.3.
Proof of the bounds for
D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\,\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast},\sigma=2
in statement (ii) in Proposition
4.2

Similar calculations show that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&A_{\Theta}(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{\lambda}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\partial_{\lambda}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&=\partial_{\lambda}f_{0k}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)+\partial_{\lambda}\Big\{A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\Big\}\\
&\quad-i(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda),\end{split}

(4.68)

and

\begin{split}&A_{\Theta}(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\Big(a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big)+ib^{\prime\prime}(y)a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&=a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\Big\{f_{0k}^{\ast}+A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\Big\}\\
&\quad+ia(y,\lambda)b^{\prime}(y)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-ia(y,\lambda)b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&\quad+\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}\Big]D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&\quad+A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.69)

Therefore,
D_{\lambda}^{2}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}
satisfies the equation for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(ib^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y)\\
&=A_{\Theta}^{-1}f_{0k}^{\ast\ast}(y,\Lambda)+\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda),\end{split}

(4.70)

where we have defined for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}f_{0k}^{\ast\ast}(y,\Lambda):=&D_{\lambda}\Big\{f_{0k}^{\ast}+A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\Big\}\\
&+\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}\Big]D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\\
&+i\Big[a(y,\lambda)b^{\prime}(y)-1\Big](\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-ia(y,\lambda)b^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.71)

Using the bounds (
4.4
) of the limiting absorption principle and the equation (
4.55
)-(
4.56
), it suffices to show that

\big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)h\big\|_{X^{1-2(2-\gamma_{2}),-\gamma_{2}+2}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}(\delta(\Lambda))^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{2})}M_{k},

(4.72)

where

h\in\Big\{A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(f_{0k}^{\ast\ast}(\cdot,\Lambda)\big)(y),\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big\}.

(4.73)

To prove the bounds (
4.72
)-(
4.73
), as in section
4.4.2
, we divide we divide into the singular regions for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|
and non-singular regions for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|
. More precisely, using the notation as in Lemma
3.4
, it suffices to prove that for
h
as in (
4.73
),

\begin{split}&\Big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)\Big[1-\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\varphi\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)\Big]h\Big\|_{X^{1-2(2-\gamma_{2}),-\gamma_{2}+2}(\mathfrak{M})}\\
&\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}(\delta(\Lambda))^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{2})}M_{k},\end{split}

(4.74)

and for
\dagger\in\{+,-\}
,

\begin{split}&\big\|\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(\Lambda)\varphi\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)h\big\|_{X^{1-2(2-\gamma_{2}),-\gamma_{2}+2}(\mathfrak{M})}\\
&\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}(\delta(\Lambda))^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{2})}M_{k}.\end{split}

(4.75)

To establish (
4.74
), using Lemma (
3.3
), (
4.11
) with
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
and equation (
4.7
) and (
4.55
)-(
4.56
), we obtain the bounds that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},\dagger\in\{+,-\}
and
h
as in (
4.73
),

\begin{split}&\Big|\Big[1-\sum_{\dagger\in\{+,-\}}\varphi\big((y-y^{\dagger}_{\lambda})/|\lambda-b(y_{j\ast})|^{1/2}\big)\Big]h(y)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma_{1}-3}\varrho_{j}^{-3}(y;\Lambda)\lesssim\delta^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{2})}\varrho_{j}^{-2+2(2-\gamma_{2})}\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma_{2}-3}(y;\Lambda).\end{split}

(4.76)

The desired bounds then follow from the estimates (
4.51
).

We now turn to the proof of (
4.75
) for
h
as in (
4.73
).
The bounds (
4.75
) for

h\in\Big\{\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu},\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big\}

(4.77)

follows from the bounds (
4.11
) with
\sigma=1
and integration by parts using the bounds on the modified Green’s function. To establish the bound (
4.74
) for
h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(f_{0k}^{\ast\ast}(\cdot,\lambda,\nu,\alpha)\big)(y)
, using the commutator relations (
4.40
) and noting that the terms associated with the commutators are easier to handle, and in view of the support property of
ab^{\prime}-1
, it suffices to prove (
4.75
) for

\begin{split}&h\in A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big\{D_{\lambda}f_{0k}^{\ast}+A_{\Theta}\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)\\
&\quad+\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}\Big]D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.78)

The bound (
4.75
) follows for

h=\Big[\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}+2\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial^{2}_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}\Big](y,\Lambda)

(4.79)

follows from the bounds on the modified Green’s function, integration by parts and the bounds (
4.4
) for
\sigma=0
. The bounds (
4.75
) for

h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big[\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}^{2}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}+2\frac{\nu}{k}\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\partial_{y}^{2}\Big]D_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)

(4.80)

follows from equation (
4.55
) using argument similar to (
4.30
)-(
4.34
). It remains to consider the term

h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}D_{\lambda}f_{0k}^{\ast}.

(4.81)

Recalling the identity (
4.56
) and skipping terms that can be bounded that are similar as before, we focus only on proving (
4.75
) for

h=A_{\Theta}^{-1}D^{2}_{\lambda}f_{0k}.

(4.82)

Recalling the formula (
4.8
), using the commutator relations (
4.40
) and focusing on the most singular terms, it suffices to consider the term

h=(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})D_{\lambda}^{2}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\omega_{0k}(y)\Big].

(4.83)

The desired bound (
4.75
) for
h
in (
4.83
) follow from simple calculations using the bounds on the modified Green’s function.

The proof of Proposition
4.2
is now complete.

5.
Proof of the main theorem

In this section we give the proof of the main theorem
1.1
, using the limiting absorption principle, see Proposition
4.2
and Corollary
4.1
, with
\gamma_{\ast}=\gamma+\frac{2-\gamma}{2}
for sufficiently small
\nu>0
depending on
\gamma
, and the representation formula (
1.19
).

As in
[
41
]
, the representation formulae (
1.19
) for
\omega_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
follow from the theory of semigroups of sectorial operators, see section 4, Chapter II of
[
21
]
. We omit the standard details and refer to
[
21
]
. The main task is to establish the following bounds.

Proposition 5.1
.

Assume that
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
and
\omega_{0k}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Define

\aleph:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{C}:\real\mu\geq-\sigma_{\sharp}|\nu/k|^{1/2}\}.

(5.1)

For any
\mu=\alpha-i\lambda\in\aleph
and
\nu\in(0,\nu_{0})
, (
1.18
) is solvable for a unique solution
\omega_{k,\nu}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
which is holomorphic in
\mu
in the interior of
\aleph
and continuous on
\aleph
. In addition, we have the bound

\|\omega_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim_{k,\nu,\omega_{0k}}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda,\alpha\rangle},

(5.2)

and

\|\partial_{\lambda}\omega_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim_{k,\nu,\omega_{0k}}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda,\alpha\rangle^{2}},

(5.3)

Proposition
5.1
follows from the limiting absorption principle, see Proposition
4.1
, which implies that there is no eigenvalue of
L_{k,\nu}
inside
\aleph
. The absence of eigenvalues inside
\aleph
is sufficient to deduce (
5.2
)-(
5.3
) since we allow the bounds to depend on
\nu
and consequently the problem is now elliptic.

We divide the proof of Theorem
1.1
into three different regions (i)
\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\gtrsim 1
, (ii)
|\nu/k|^{1/4}\ll\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\ll 1
, and (iii)
\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\ll|\nu/k|^{1/4}
. We assume that
t\geq 1
as the case of
0<t<1
follows from the local wellposedness of the Navier-Stokes equations.

Denote

M_{k}:=\big\|\omega_{0k}\big\|_{H^{3}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})},

(5.4)

and recall the definition (
4.21
) for
a(y,\lambda)
with
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

a(y,\lambda):=\Big[1-\varphi\big(\frac{y-y_{1\ast}}{\delta_{2}(\lambda)}\big)\Big]\Big[1-\varphi\big(\frac{y-y_{2\ast}}{\delta_{2}(\lambda)}\big)\Big]\frac{1}{b^{\prime}(y)}.

(5.5)

We shall use the following formulae (recall also (
4.9
) for the derivative
D_{\lambda}
),

\begin{split}\partial_{\lambda}&=D_{\lambda}-a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y},\\
\partial_{y}\partial_{\lambda}&=\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}-\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}-a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2},\\
\partial_{\lambda}^{2}&=D_{\lambda}^{2}-2a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}D_{\lambda}-\partial_{\lambda}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}+a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}a(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}+a^{2}(y,\lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.6)

The main tools are Proposition
4.2
, Corollary
4.1
, and the representation formula (
1.19
). More specifically we use the identities, with
\alpha=-\sigma_{\sharp}|\nu/k|^{1/2}
, that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
t\geq 1
,

\psi_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi k^{2}t^{2}}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\partial_{\lambda}^{2}\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda,

(5.7)

\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)=-\frac{1}{2i\pi kt}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\partial_{y}\partial_{\lambda}\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda,

(5.8)

and

\omega_{k}(t,y)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda.

(5.9)

To prove Theorem
1.1
, it suffices to show that for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
and
t\geq 1
,

|\psi_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim\frac{|y-y_{1\ast}|^{\gamma-2}+|y-y_{2\ast}|^{\gamma-2}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}e^{-\sigma_{\sharp}\nu^{1/2}t}|k|^{-7/4}M_{k},

(5.10)

|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim e^{-\sigma_{\sharp}\nu^{1/2}t}\langle t\rangle^{-1}|k|^{-3/4}M_{k},

(5.11)

and

\big|\omega_{k}(t,y)\big|\lesssim\Big[\big(|y-y_{j\ast}|+|\nu|^{1/4}\big)^{\gamma}+t^{-\gamma/2}\Big]e^{-\sigma_{\sharp}\nu^{1/2}t}|k|^{-1/2-(2-\gamma)/10}M_{k}.

(5.12)

We shall give the detailed proofs for (
5.10
) and (
5.12
) only, as the proof of (
5.11
) is identical to that of (
5.7
). We only remark that
\partial_{\lambda}\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)
is
less singular
than
\partial_{\lambda}^{2}\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)
in the region
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}},j\in\{1,2\}
. Roughly speaking, when
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, taking one derivative in
y
costs a factor
\varrho_{j,k}^{-1}
while taking a derivative in
\lambda
costs a larger factor of
\varrho_{j,k}^{-1}\varrho_{j}^{-1}
.

Since the desired bounds (
5.10
) and (
5.12
) are pointwise, in view of the nature of the singularities of the integrand in (
5.7
) and (
5.9
), it is convenient to divide the integrals (
5.7
) and (
5.9
) into the sum of a local integral and a nonlocal integral, as follows. For
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we define

\begin{split}&\psi_{k{\rm loc}}(t,y)\\
&:=\frac{1}{2\pi k^{2}t^{2}}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\,|\lambda-b(y)|\ll\,\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}|b(y)-b(y_{j\ast})|+|\nu/k|^{1/2}\}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\partial_{\lambda}^{2}\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda,\end{split}

(5.13)

\begin{split}&\psi_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)\\
&:=\frac{1}{2\pi k^{2}t^{2}}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\,|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim\,\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}|b(y)-b(y_{j\ast})|+|\nu/k|^{1/2}\}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\partial_{\lambda}^{2}\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda.\end{split}

(5.14)

Similarly, we define, for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\begin{split}&\omega_{k{\rm loc}}(t,y)\\
&:=-\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\,|\lambda-b(y)|\ll\,\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}|b(y)-b(y_{j\ast})|+|\nu/k|^{1/2}\}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda,\end{split}

(5.15)

\begin{split}&\omega_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)\\
&:=-\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{\alpha|k|t}e^{-\nu k^{2}t}\int_{\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\,|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim\,\min_{j\in\{1,2\}}|b(y)-b(y_{j\ast})|+|\nu/k|^{1/2}\}}e^{-ik\lambda t}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\,d\lambda.\end{split}

(5.16)

To establish (
5.10
) and (
5.12
), it suffices to prove that for
h\in\{\psi_{k{\rm loc}}(t,y),\psi_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)\}
and
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},t\geq 1
, we have the bounds

|h(t,y)|\lesssim\frac{|y-y_{1\ast}|^{\gamma-2}+|y-y_{2\ast}|^{\gamma-2}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}e^{-\sigma_{\sharp}\nu^{1/2}t}|k|^{-7/4}M_{k},

(5.17)

and that
h\in\{\omega_{k{\rm loc}}(t,y),\omega_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)\}
and
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},t\geq 1
, we have the bounds for
j\in\{1,2\}
,

\big|h(t,y)\big|\lesssim\Big[\big(|y-y_{j\ast}|+|\nu|^{1/4}\big)^{\gamma}+t^{-\gamma/2}\Big]e^{-\sigma_{\sharp}\nu^{1/2}t}|k|^{-1/2-(2-\gamma)/10}M_{k}.

(5.18)

We divide the proof of (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) into several cases, depending on the location of
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
relative to the critical points
y_{1\ast},y_{2\ast}
.

5.1.
The region
\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\gtrsim 1
.

We firstly summarize the following bounds for
\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)
, which follow from Proposition
4.2
and Corollary
4.1
. For
\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\gtrsim 1
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
and
\sigma\in\{0,1,2\}
, we have the bounds

\|D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(\cdot,\Lambda)\|_{H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim|k|^{-5/2+\sigma}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-1-\sigma}M_{k}.

(5.19)

Additionally, for
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
with
j\in\{1,2\}
,
\beta\in\{0,1\}
and
\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\gtrsim 1
, we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lr}|k|^{-\beta}|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D_{\lambda}^{\sigma}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim|k|^{-\gamma_{\ast}-1/2+\sigma}M_{k},&\quad{\rm if}\,\,\sigma\in\{0,1\},\\
|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D_{\lambda}^{2}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim|k|^{-\gamma_{\ast}+3/2+\beta}\big(\delta(\lambda)\big)^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{\ast})}M_{k},&\quad{\rm if}\,\,\sigma=2.\end{array}

(5.20)

The bounds (
5.19
)-(
5.20
), together with (
4.15
)-(
4.16
) and the identities (
5.6
) are sufficient for proving the desired estimates bounds (
5.17
) for
h=\psi_{k{\rm nloc}}
and (
5.20
) for
\omega_{k{\rm nloc}}
.

To prove the bounds (
5.17
) for
h=\psi_{k{\rm loc}}
, we use (
4.16
), the identity (
5.6
), and with a slight abuse of notation denoting
\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\lambda)
as
\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)
to emphasize the dependence on
\lambda
, to obtain that

\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\lambda)-\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,b(y))=\int_{b(y)}^{\lambda}\Big[D_{\tau}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\tau)-a(y,\tau)\partial_{y}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\tau)\Big]\,d\tau

(5.21)

and for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll 1
that

\begin{split}&\bigg|\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)-\frac{\omega_{0k}(y)-ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,b(y))}{i(\lambda-b(y))+|\nu/k|^{1/3}}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\nu/k|^{-1/3}}{1+|\nu/k|^{-2/3}|\lambda-b(y)|^{2}}|k|^{-5/2}M_{k}+\frac{1}{|\lambda-b(y)|^{1/2}}|k|^{-3/2}M_{k}+|k|^{-1}M_{k}.\end{split}

(5.22)

The desired bounds then follow from (
5.22
) and the identity

(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\psi_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)=\omega^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda).

(5.23)

The bounds (
5.20
) for
\omega_{k{\rm loc}}
follow from (
5.22
).

5.2.
The region
|\nu/k|^{1/4}\ll\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\ll 1
.

For the sake of concreteness we assume that
|y-y_{1\ast}|\in[m/2,m]
and
|\nu/k|^{1/4}\ll m\ll 1
. It follows from proposition
4.2
that the following inequalities hold. For
\sigma\in\{0,1\},\beta\in\{0,1\}
,

•

If
|\lambda-b(y_{1\ast})|^{1/2}\ll m
, then

|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim|y-y_{1\ast}|^{-\sigma}\big(|y-y_{1\ast}|\wedge\frac{1}{|k|}\big)^{\gamma_{\ast}-\sigma-\beta}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k};

(5.24)

•

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
and
|\lambda-b(y_{1\ast})|^{1/2}\gtrsim m
, then

\|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\|_{L^{2}(m/4,4m)}\lesssim\delta(\lambda)^{1/2-\sigma}\big(\delta(\lambda)\wedge\frac{1}{|k|}\big)^{\gamma_{\ast}-\sigma-\beta}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda\rangle^{1+\sigma}}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k};

(5.25)

•

If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
, then

|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim|k|^{-\gamma_{\ast}-1/2+\sigma+\beta}M_{k}.

(5.26)

For
\sigma=2,\beta\in\{0,1\}
, we have the following bounds.

•

If
|\lambda-b(y_{1\ast})|^{1/2}\ll m
, then

\begin{split}&|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\\
&\lesssim(\delta(\lambda))^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{\ast})}|y-y_{1\ast}|^{-1+2(2-\gamma_{\ast})}\big(|y-y_{1\ast}|\wedge\frac{1}{|k|}\big)^{\gamma_{\ast}-2-\beta}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k};\end{split}

(5.27)

•

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}},|\lambda-b(y_{1\ast})|^{1/2}\gtrsim m
, then

\|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\|_{L^{2}(m/4,4m)}\lesssim\delta(\lambda)^{-3/2}\big(\delta(\lambda)\wedge\frac{1}{|k|}\big)^{\gamma_{\ast}-2-\beta}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda\rangle^{3}}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k};

(5.28)

•

If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
, then

|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim(\delta(\lambda))^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{\ast})}|k|^{-\gamma_{\ast}+3/2+\beta}M_{k}.

(5.29)

The desired bounds (
5.17
) for
h=\psi_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)
follow from the bounds (
5.24
)-(
5.29
), by straightforward calculations, using the formula (
5.14
).

To prove (
5.17
) for
h=\psi_{k{\rm loc}}(t,y)
and (
5.18
), we use the identity (
5.23
), the bound (
4.14
) which we rewrite as follows. Denoting
\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)
as
\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\lambda)
with a slight abuse of notation to emphasize the dependence on
\lambda
, for
\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll\,|b(y)-b(y_{1\ast})|
, we have the bounds

\begin{split}&\bigg|\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)+\frac{ib^{\prime\prime}(y)\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,b(y))}{i(\lambda-b(y))+|\nu/k|^{1/3}|y-y_{1\ast}|^{2/3}}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\varrho_{1,k}^{\gamma_{\ast}-1/2}(y;\Lambda)|y-y_{1\ast}|^{-1/2}\frac{|\nu/k|^{-1/3}|y-y_{1\ast}|^{1/3}}{1+(|\nu/k|^{1/3}|y-y_{1\ast}|^{2/3})^{-2}|\lambda-b(y)|^{2}}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}\\
&\quad+\varrho_{1,k}^{\gamma_{\ast}-3/2}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{1}^{1/2}(y;\Lambda)|\lambda-b(y)|^{-1/2}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.\end{split}

(5.30)

The bounds (
5.18
) for
h=\omega_{k{\rm loc}}(t,y)
follow from (
5.30
).

To prove (
5.18
) with
h=\omega_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)
, we use also the following bounds. In view of (
4.53
) and Proposition
4.2
, we have the pointwise estimates for
\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\gtrsim|b(y)-b(y_{1\ast})|\}
,

\begin{split}\big|\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\big|&=\Big|\omega_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)+(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2})\Big\{\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi\big((y-y_{j\ast})/\delta_{0}\big)\frac{\omega_{0k}(y)}{ib^{\prime\prime}(y)}\Big\}\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma_{\ast}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{j}^{-2}(y;\Lambda)|k|^{-1/2}M_{k},\end{split}

(5.31)

|\partial_{\lambda}\omega_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim\varrho_{j,k}^{\gamma_{\ast}-3/2}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{j}^{-5/2}(y;\Lambda)|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.

(5.32)

Using the identity

\partial_{\lambda}\omega_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)=\partial_{\lambda}\omega_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)

(5.33)

and equation (
1.18
) we get that
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\big(\cup_{j=1}^{2}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big)
,

|\partial_{\lambda}\omega_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\lambda\rangle^{2}}|k|^{-3/2}M_{k}.

(5.34)

For
\lambda\in\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
, using (
4.53
) and Proposition
4.2
we get that

|\partial_{\lambda}\omega_{k,\nu}^{\ast}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}M_{k}.

(5.35)

Fix
\tau\in(1,\infty)
as a parameter to be chosen below. We divide the integral (
5.16
) into three regions for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
: (I)
\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}
with
|b(y)-b(y_{1\ast})|\lesssim|\lambda-b(y)|<\tau^{2}|b(y)-b(y_{1\ast})|
; (II)
\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}
with
|\lambda-b(y)|\geq\tau^{2}|b(y)-b(y_{1\ast})|
; (III)
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\big[\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}\cup\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}\big]
; (IV)
\lambda\in\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
.

Using the bounds (
5.31
)-(
5.35
), and integrating by parts in
\lambda
in the regions (II)-(IV), we obtain the bounds for
t\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&|\omega_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)|\lesssim\bigg[|y-y_{1\ast}|^{\gamma}+\tau^{\gamma}|y-y_{1\ast}|^{\gamma}+\frac{1}{|k|t}\tau^{\gamma-2}|y-y_{1\ast}|^{\gamma-2}\bigg]|k|^{-1/2-(2-\gamma)/10}M_{k}.\end{split}

(5.36)

Optimizing in
\tau\in(1,\infty)
, the desired bounds for (
5.18
) with
h=\omega_{k{\rm nloc}}(t,y)
follows.

5.3.
The region
\min\{|y-y_{1\ast}|,|y-y_{2\ast}|\}\lesssim|\nu/k|^{1/4}
.

For the sake of concreteness, we assume that
|y-y_{1\ast}|\lesssim|\nu/k|^{1/4}
. In this case we shall use the following bounds, which are consequences of Proposition
4.2
.

•

If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}
,
\sigma\in\{0,1,2\},\beta\in\{0,1\}
and
\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}
,

\begin{split}&\|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)\|_{L^{2}(|y-y_{1\ast}|\leq 3\delta(\Lambda)}\lesssim(\delta(\Lambda))^{1/2-\sigma}\Big[\delta(\Lambda)\wedge\frac{1}{|k|}\Big]^{\gamma_{\ast}-\sigma-\beta}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k};\end{split}

(5.37)

•

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
,
\sigma\in\{0,1,2\}
and
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,

|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim\langle\lambda\rangle^{-1-\sigma}|k|^{-5/2+\sigma+\beta}M_{k};

(5.38)

•

If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
,
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
and
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,

|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{\sigma}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim|k|^{-\gamma_{\ast}+\sigma+\beta}|k|^{-1/2}M_{k};

(5.39)

•

If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}
,
\sigma=2
and
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,

|\partial_{y}^{\beta}D^{2}_{\lambda}\psi^{\ast}_{k,\nu}(y,\Lambda)|\lesssim\big(\delta(\Lambda)\big)^{-1-2(2-\gamma_{\ast})}|k|^{-\gamma_{\ast}+2+\beta}.

(5.40)

This case is somewhat simpler in fact, since, roughly speaking, we can use the ellipticity of the Orr-Sommerfeld equation to bound derivatives without much loss, using Lemma
3.3
. Indeed, the desired bounds (
5.17
)-(
5.18
) follow from an analogous argument as in section
5.2
, using (
5.37
)-(
5.40
), but somewhat simpler since there is no need to separately treat the region
|\lambda-b(y)|\ll|\nu/k|^{1/2}
which is no longer singular. We omit the details.
Theorem
1.1
is now proved.

6.
Proof of the limiting absorption principle (I): non-degenerate region

In this section we give the proof of the limiting absorption principle in the non-degenerate region when
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\big(\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}\cup\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}\big)
or
\alpha\geq 1
. We begin with some technical bounds on various operators that are useful below, and then prove the limiting absorption principle using a compactness argument.

Denote
\epsilon:=\nu/k
. Recall that
\epsilon\in(0,1/8]
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
, and

(\alpha,\lambda)\in\Big[(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},\infty)\times\big(\mathbb{R}\setminus(\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}\cup\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}})\big)\Big]\cup\Big[(1,\infty)\times\big(\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}\cup\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}\big)\Big].

We also recall the definition (
4.1
) for
T_{\Theta}
and set for simplicity of notations
\Delta_{k}:=\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2}
.

6.1.
Preliminary bounds

We define two regions in the
(\alpha,\lambda)
plane

\displaystyle R_{nd}

\displaystyle:=\big\{(\alpha,\lambda)\,|\,\alpha\geq-\sigma_{0}|\epsilon|^{1/2},\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}\cup\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}})\big\},

(6.1)

\displaystyle R_{\alpha}

\displaystyle:=\big\{(\alpha,\lambda)\,|\,\alpha\geq 1,\lambda\in\Sigma_{1,\delta_{0}}\cup\Sigma_{2,\delta_{0}}\big\}.

(6.2)

In
R_{nd}
, which we call the “non-degenerate region”,
\lambda
is separated from the critical points; in
R_{\alpha}
, which we call the “
\alpha
dominated region”,
\alpha\geq 1
. Therefore, there is no singularity around the critical points
y_{1\ast},y_{2\ast}
in either case when we apply the operator
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
.

Throughout the section we assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in R_{nd}\cup R_{\alpha}
.
We begin with the following pointwise bounds.

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose that

m:=\inf_{y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(|b(y)-\lambda|+|\alpha|+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{3}})\gtrsim 1.

Then for any bounded function
f
on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\Big|\frac{1}{k^{\beta}}\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{\Theta}f(y)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}}{k^{2}m},\quad{\rm with}\,\,\beta\in\{0,1\}.

(6.3)

Proof.

The proof is a consequence (
2.3
), Proposition
3.1
in the non-degenerate regime, and the fact that
ke^{-k|y|}
is an approximation of the identity.
∎

The bounds from Lemma
6.1
are sufficient for our purposes when
(\alpha,\lambda)\in R_{\alpha}
. For
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
such that

|b(y)-\lambda|+|\alpha|+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\ll 1,

we need to carry out a finer analysis of the behavior near the critical layer.
To begin with, we decompose
T_{\Theta}
as follows. Let
\varphi_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-1,1)
with
\varphi_{0}\equiv 1
on
[-1/2,1/2]
.
Then for any number
L>0
, define for
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\varphi_{L}(y):=\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{y}{L}\right).

(6.4)

We now decompose for any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
,

\displaystyle T_{\Theta}f

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{2}T_{\Theta,\ell}f

(6.5)

where

\displaystyle T_{\Theta,0}f(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}G_{k}(y,z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(z)\Big[1-\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\varphi_{\theta}(z-y_{\ell})\Big]\,dz

(6.6)

and

\displaystyle T_{\Theta,\ell}f(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}G_{k}(y,z)\varphi_{\theta}(z-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(z)\,dz

(6.7)

where
b(y_{\ell})=\lambda,\ell\in\{1,2\}
and
\theta=\theta(\delta_{0})>0
is a sufficiently small constant.
We note that for
\lambda
in the interior of
b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, the set
b^{-1}(\lambda)
has cardinality 2.

Since the argument of
T_{\Theta,0}
is supported away from the critical layer we have the following pointwise bounds.

Lemma 6.2
.

There exists
\theta_{0}\in(0,1)
, depending only on
b(y)
, such that for
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0}]
and any
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
we have the following bounds:

\frac{1}{k^{\beta}}\big|\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{\Theta,0}f(y)\big|\lesssim\frac{\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}}{k^{2}},\quad\beta\in\{0,1,2\}.

(6.8)

Proof.

Proposition
3.1
implies that

\Big|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(z)\big(1-\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\varphi_{\theta}^{2}(z-y_{\ell})\big)\Big|\lesssim_{\theta_{0}}\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

(6.9)

The proof is then an immediate consequence of Lemma
2.1
and the fact that
ke^{-k|y|}
is an approximation of identity.
∎

We now turn our attention to
T_{\Theta,\ell},\ell\in\{1,2\}
. The following result is useful in characterizing the singular behavior near the critical layer.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists
\theta_{0}\in(0,1)
, such that for
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0}],\,\ell\in\{1,2\}
, the following statement holds.
Suppose that
f\in H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
with

M:=\norm{f}_{H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}.

(6.10)

We view the function
\varphi_{\theta}(y-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)

as a function on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then

\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathcal{F}\left(\varphi_{\theta}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)\right)(\xi)\big|\lesssim M,

(6.11)

where
\mathcal{F}(h)
denotes the Fourier transform of
h
and
y_{\ell}
satisfies
b(y_{\ell})=\lambda
.

Proof.

With a slight abuse of notation, denote
\varphi_{\ell}(y):=\varphi\big(\frac{2(y-y_{\ell})}{\theta}\big)
, for
\ell\in\{1,2\},y\in\mathbb{R}
. Define also
w_{c,\ell}
as the solution to

\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}w_{c,\ell}(y)-\alpha w_{c,\ell}(y)+ib^{\prime}(y_{\ell})(y_{\ell}-y)w_{c,\ell}(y)=f(y_{\ell}),\quad y\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.12)

We can solve
w_{c,\ell}
explicitly as

w_{c,\ell}(y,y_{\ell})=\frac{\ f(y_{\ell})}{b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})}\left(\frac{b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}W_{\ell}\left(\left(\frac{b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}(y-y_{\ell})\right),

(6.13)

where

\widehat{W}_{\ell}(\xi)=-\sqrt{2\pi}\exp\left(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}+\frac{\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}\alpha\xi}{(b^{\prime}(y_{\ell}))^{\frac{2}{3}}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,0]}(\xi),\quad\xi\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.14)

Define
w_{R}:\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\mathbb{C}
as

\displaystyle w_{R}:=A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)-\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\varphi_{\ell}w_{c,\ell},

(6.15)

where we view
\varphi_{\ell}w_{c,\ell}
as functions on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
It follows from (
6.12
) and (
6.15
) that
w_{R}
satisfies the equation for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\displaystyle\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}w_{R}(y)-\alpha w_{R}(y)+i(\lambda-b(y))w_{R}(y)

\displaystyle=f(y)-\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\varphi_{\ell}(y)f(y_{\ell})-\epsilon\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\Big[\partial_{y}\varphi_{\ell}(y)\partial_{y}w_{c,\ell}(y)+\partial_{y}^{2}\varphi_{\ell}(y)w_{c,\ell}(y)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+i\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\varphi_{\ell}(y)w_{c,\ell}(y)\Big[b(y_{\ell})+b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})(y-y_{\ell})-b(y)\Big].

(6.16)

In view of Proposition
3.1
, we conclude that for

y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

|w_{R}(y)|\lesssim\frac{M\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{6}}}{\langle\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\alpha|,\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}(b(y)-\lambda)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(6.17)

Therefore,

\big|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{\theta}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f))(\xi)\big|\lesssim M,\quad\ell\in\{1,2\},

(6.18)

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

Note that
\varphi_{\theta}(y-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)
can also be viewed as a periodic function on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then the Fourier coefficients
\big[\varphi_{\theta}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)\big]_{l}
are bounded up to an implicit constant by
M
,

\sup_{l\in\frac{2\pi\mathbb{Z}}{\mathfrak{p}}}\Big|\big[\varphi_{\theta}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)\big]_{l}\Big|\lesssim M.

(6.19)

Recalling the definition of
T_{\Theta,\ell}f,\ell\in\{1,2\}
as
\Delta_{k}^{-1}\varphi_{\theta}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(ib^{\prime\prime}f)
and the Fourier multiplier representation of
\Delta_{k}^{-1}
, we have as a consequence of Lemma
6.3
the following bounds for Fourier coefficients of
T_{\Theta,\ell}f
:

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
\norm{f}_{H_{k}^{1}}=M
. Then we have

\big|[T_{\Theta,\ell}f]_{l}\big|\lesssim\frac{M}{l^{2}+k^{2}},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z},\,l\in\frac{2\pi\mathbb{Z}}{\mathfrak{p}}.

(6.20)

Finally, combining Lemma
6.1
, Lemma
6.2
and Corollary
6.1
, we can conclude the following bounds for
T_{\Theta}
on
H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Lemma 6.4
.

T_{\Theta}:H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
satisfies the bounds

\norm{T_\Theta}_{H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to H^{1}_{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim k^{-1/2}.

(6.21)

Moreover, the family of operators
T_{\Theta}
has the following “uniform compactness” property. More precisely, assume that
\ell,k_{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
and that
f_{\ell}
is a sequence of functions satisfying
\norm{f_\ell}_{H_{k_{\ell}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}=1
. Let

\Theta_{\ell}:=(\epsilon_{\ell},\alpha_{\ell},k_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell})\in\big(0,1/8\big)\times(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2},1)\times\big(\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)\big)\times(\Sigma_{0}\setminus\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}).

Then for any
k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
, the sequence

T_{\Theta_{\ell}}(f_{\ell})

(6.22)

has a convergent subsequnece in
H_{k_{0}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

For applications below, we need to study the singular behavior of
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)
as
\epsilon\to 0
, which is useful to connect the spectral assumption on the invisicd Rayleigh operator to the viscous Orr-Sommerfed operator.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
f\in H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Then
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)
has the following limiting behavior for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
in the sense of distributions.

•

If
\alpha_{0}\neq 0
or
\lambda_{0}\notin b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to\alpha_{0},\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)=\frac{-f(y)}{\alpha_{0}+i(b(y)-\lambda_{0})};

(6.23)

•

If
\lambda_{0}\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})\setminus\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)={\rm P.V.}\frac{-f(y)}{i(b(y)-\lambda_{0})}-\pi\sum_{b(z)=\lambda_{0}}\frac{f(z)}{|b^{\prime}(z)|}\delta(y-z).

(6.24)

Proof.

We only give the detailed proof of (
6.24
), since the other case is easier, and indicate the necessary changes for the proof of (
6.23
) at the end of the proof. Fix an even cutoff function
\Phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-2,2)
with
\Phi\equiv 1
on
[-1,1]
. Let
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
be a test function. We have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{b(z)=\lambda}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{b(z)=\lambda}\left(1-\Phi\left(\frac{y-z}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)\right)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy.

(6.25)

For
y
in the support of
1-\Phi
, we divide (
3.34
) by
i(b(y)-\lambda)
to get that

A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)=-\frac{f(y)}{i(b(y)-\lambda)}-\frac{\alpha A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)}{i(b(y)-\lambda)}+\frac{\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)}{i(b(y)-\lambda)}.

(6.26)

Proposition
3.1
then implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{b(z)=\lambda}\left(1-\Phi\left(\frac{y-z}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)\right)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy=-\text{P.V.}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{f(y)\phi(y)}{i(b(y)-\lambda_{0})}\,dy.

(6.27)

On the other hand, recalling the decomposition (
6.15
) in Lemma
6.3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{b(z)=\lambda}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{\ell}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{c,\ell}(y)\phi(y)\,dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{\ell}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{R}(y)\phi(y)\,dy.

(6.28)

From (
6.17
), it follows that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{\ell}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{R}(y)\phi(y)\,dy=0.

(6.29)

We now focus our attention on
w_{c,1}
with the treatment of
w_{c,2}
being identical. Since
w_{c,1}
is defined on
\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{1}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{c,1}(y)\phi(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\phi(z_{1})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{1}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{c,1}(y)\,dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{1}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{c,1}(y)(\phi(y)-\phi(z_{1}))\,dy.

(6.30)

Since
w_{c,1}(y)(\phi(y)-\phi(z_{1}))
is bounded, the second term converges to 0 as
\alpha,\epsilon\to 0
. Therefore, it suffices to consider the first term. In view of (
6.13
), we get that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{1}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)w_{c,1}(y)\,dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{1}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)\frac{\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}f(z_{1})}{(b^{\prime}(z_{1}))^{\frac{2}{3}}}W(\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}b^{\prime}(z_{1})(y-z_{1}))\,dy.

(6.31)

By rescaling and Parseval’s identity, we obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi\left(\frac{y-z_{1}}{(|\alpha|+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)\frac{\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}f(z_{1})}{(b^{\prime}(z_{1}))^{\frac{2}{3}}}W(\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}b^{\prime}(z_{1})(y-z_{1}))\,dy

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{2\pi}\varphi(z_{1})\frac{f(z_{1})}{|b^{\prime}(z_{1})|}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}(\xi)d\xi=-\pi\varphi(z_{1})\frac{f(z_{1})}{|b^{\prime}(z_{1})|}.

(6.32)

We now turn to the case where
\lambda_{0}\notin b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\alpha_{0}=0
. For
\lambda
sufficiently close to
\lambda_{0}
,
b(y)-\lambda
does not vanish on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Hence, assuming that
\lambda\notin b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, we can write that

A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)=-\frac{f(y)}{\alpha+i(b(y)-\lambda)}+\frac{\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)}{\alpha+i(b(y)-\lambda)}.

(6.33)

Proposition
3.1
implies

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to 0,\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{f(y)\phi(y)}{i(b(y)-\lambda_{0})}\,dy.

(6.34)

Finally, assume that
\alpha_{0}\neq 0
and
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. In this case we again write
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)
as in (
6.33
) by taking
\alpha
sufficiently close to
\alpha_{0}
so that
\alpha\neq 0
. It then follows from Proposition
3.1
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0,\,\alpha\to\alpha_{0},\,\lambda\to\lambda_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\phi(y)\,dy=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{f(y)\phi(y)}{\alpha_{0}+i(b(y)-\lambda_{0})}\,dy,

(6.35)

which completes the proof of Lemma
6.5
.
∎

6.2.
The compactness argument

We are now ready to prove the limiting absorption in the non-degenerate case.

Proposition 6.1
.

There exist
\kappa>0
and
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1/8)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
, and
(\alpha,\lambda)\in R_{nd}\cup R_{\alpha}
,
we have the bounds

\norm{ \psi+ T_{\Theta} (i b'' \psi)}_{H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\geq\kappa\norm{\psi}_{H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})},

(6.36)

for any
\psi\in H_{k}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Proof.

Suppose (
6.36
