) does not hold. Then there exists a sequence
(\psi_{\ell},\kappa_{\ell},\Theta_{\ell})
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
with
(\kappa_{\ell},\epsilon_{\ell})\to 0+
,
\norm{\psi_\ell}_{H_{k_{\ell}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}=1
, such that

\norm{\psi_\ell+ T_{\Theta_\ell} \psi_\ell}_{H_{k_{\ell}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\leq\kappa_{\ell}.

(6.37)

We first assume that
(\alpha_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell})\in R_{nd}
.
By Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.4
we see that
(k_{\ell},\alpha_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell})
are bounded. Therefore, passing to a subsequence if necessary, we can assume that

(\alpha_{\ell},k_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell})\to(\alpha_{0},k_{0},\lambda_{0})\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)\times\overline{\mathbb{R}\setminus(\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}}).

It also follows from Lemma
6.4
that
T_{\Theta_{\ell}}(ib^{\prime\prime}\psi_{\ell})
is relatively compact in
H_{k_{0}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Writing
\psi_{\ell}
as

\psi_{\ell}=\psi_{\ell}+T_{\Theta_{\ell}}(ib^{\prime\prime}\psi_{\ell})-T_{\Theta_{\ell}}(ib^{\prime\prime}\psi_{\ell})

(6.38)

and passing to a subsequence if necessary, we can infer that
\psi_{\ell}
converges
strongly
in
H_{k_{0}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
to
\Psi\in H_{k_{0}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
with
\|\Psi\|_{H_{k_{0}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}=1
.

As a consequence of (
6.37
), we conclude that
\Psi
satisfies the equation that

\Psi+\lim_{\ell\to\infty}T_{\Theta_{\ell}}(ib^{\prime\prime}\Psi)=0.

(6.39)

Since

\lim_{\ell\to\infty}T_{\Theta_{\ell}}(ib^{\prime\prime}\Psi)=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\Delta_{k_{0}}^{-1}A_{\Theta_{\ell}}(ib^{\prime\prime}\Psi),

(6.40)

applying
-\Delta_{k_{0}}
yields

-\Delta_{k_{0}}\Psi-\lim_{\ell\to\infty}A_{\epsilon_{\ell}}^{-1}(ib^{\prime\prime}\Psi)=0,

(6.41)

in the sense of distributions.
Assuming that
\alpha_{0}=0
, and using Lemma
6.5
, we obtain that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

-\Delta_{k_{0}}\Psi(y)+\text{P.V.}\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(y)\Psi(y)}{b(y)-\lambda_{0}}+i\pi\sum_{b(z)=\lambda_{0}}\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(z)\Psi(z)}{|b^{\prime}(z)|}\delta(y-z)=0,

(6.42)

which contradicts the main spectral assumption (
1.1
).

In the case when
\alpha_{0}\neq 0
, we have

-\Delta_{k_{0}}\Psi(y)+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(y)\Psi(y)}{b(y)-\lambda_{0}-i\alpha_{0}}=0,

(6.43)

which would imply the existence of a discrete eigenvalue for the Rayleigh operator which also contradicts the main spectral Assumption
1.1
.

Finally, assume that
(\alpha_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell})\in R_{\alpha}
and therefore
(\alpha_{0},\lambda_{0})\in[1,\infty)\times\overline{(\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}})}
. Since
\alpha_{0}\neq 0
, we can use Lemma
6.5
to once again obtain (
6.43
), which leads to another contradiction. The proof of Proposition
6.1
is now complete.
∎

7.
Proof of the limiting absorption principle (II): the degenerate region

In this section, we consider the case where
\lambda\in\cup_{j\in\{1,2\}}\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
and
\alpha
is small. More precisely,
we define a region
R_{dj}
in the
(\alpha,\lambda)
plane as

\ R_{dj}:=\big\{(\alpha,\lambda)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,|\,\alpha\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1),\,\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\big\},\quad j\in\{1,2\},

(7.1)

where we recall that
\sigma_{0}
is chosen in Proposition
3.1
. In contrast to the case when
(\alpha,\lambda)\in R_{nd}\cup R_{\alpha}
,
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
is now “quadratically” singular near the critical layers,
b(y)=\lambda
, using the heuristic in (
1.28
).
Therefore
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
is of the same order as
\Delta_{k}
and we can no longer treat
T_{\Theta}=\Delta_{k}^{-1}A_{\Theta}^{-1}
perturbatively. To address this issue, we need to incorporate part of the nonlocal term in the main terms and prove various weighted estimates, before we can carry out an analogous compactness argument as in the non-degenerate case to prove the limiting absorption argument.

7.1.
Bounds on the operator
T_{\Theta}
.

While the definition of
T_{\Theta}
given in (
4.2
) is concise, it obscures the important cancellation between
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(ib^{\prime\prime}\psi)
and the potential
b^{\prime\prime}(y)(b(y)-\lambda-i\alpha)^{-1}
. Therefore, we expand
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(ib^{\prime\prime}\psi)
further which leads to the following decomposition for
T_{\Theta}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume that
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
,
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}},\,j\in\{1,2\}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. For
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
we can decompose for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

T_{\Theta}f(y)=-iT_{I1}f(y)-iT_{I2}f(y)-iT_{v1}f(y)-T_{v2}f(y),

(7.2)

where

\displaystyle T_{I1}f(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}\left[\Big(1-\varphi_{0}\Big(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta_{0}}\Big)\Big)b^{\prime\prime}f\right](z)\,dz,

(7.3a)

\displaystyle T_{I2}f(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}\Big(\varphi_{0}\Big(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\Big)b^{\prime\prime}f\Big)(z)\,dz,

(7.3b)

\displaystyle T_{v1}f(y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{(z-y_{j*})}{\delta(\Lambda)/3}\right)A_{\Theta}^{-1}\left[\Big(\varphi_{0}\Big(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta_{0}}\Big)-\varphi_{0}\Big(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\Big)\Big)b^{\prime\prime}f\right](z)\,dz,

(7.3c)

\displaystyle T_{v2}f(y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{j}(y,z;\Lambda)\left(1-\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{(z-y_{j*})}{\delta(\Lambda)/3}\right)\right)\frac{\epsilon\partial_{z}^{2}A_{\Theta}^{-1}\left[\left(\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta_{0}}\right)-\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\right)\right)b^{\prime\prime}f\right](z)}{b(z)-\lambda-i\alpha}\,dz.

(7.3d)

where
\varphi_{0}
is the same as in the modified Green’s function, see (
2.7
).

Remark 7.1
.

The motivation for the subscripts
I
and
v
are “inviscid” and “viscous” respectively. Both
T_{I1}
and
T_{I2}
have analogs that appear in the inviscid case (see
[
38
]
) but
T_{v1}
and
T_{v2}
only appear when viscosity is present.

Proof.

For
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
we write

\displaystyle A_{\Theta}^{-1}(ib^{\prime\prime}f)=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big[(1-\eta_{j,\delta})ib^{\prime\prime}g\big]+A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big[\eta_{j,\delta}ib^{\prime\prime}f\big],

(7.4)

where for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\eta_{j,\delta}(y):=\varphi\Big(\frac{y-y_{j\ast}}{\delta_{0}}\Big)-\varphi\Big(\frac{y-y_{j\ast}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\Big).

(7.5)

It follows from (
3.34
) that for
y
away from the critical layer we have

A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{j,\delta}ib^{\prime\prime}f)(y)=-\frac{\eta_{j,\delta}(y)b^{\prime\prime}(y)f(y)}{b(y)-\lambda-i\alpha}+\frac{\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{j,\delta}b^{\prime\prime}f)(y)}{b(y)-\lambda-i\alpha},

(7.6)

therefore,

\displaystyle\Big(1-\varphi\Big(\frac{y-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)/3}\Big)\Big)\left[A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(\eta_{j,\delta}ib^{\prime\prime}f\big)(z)+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(y)}{b(y)-\lambda-i\alpha}\Big[\varphi\Big(\frac{z-y_{j\ast}}{\delta_{0}}\Big)-\varphi\Big(\frac{z-y_{j\ast}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\Big)\Big]f(z)\right]

\displaystyle=\left(1-\varphi\Big(\frac{y-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)/3}\Big)\right)\frac{\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{j,\delta}b^{\prime\prime}f)(y)}{b(y)-\lambda-i\alpha},

(7.7)

and

\displaystyle\varphi\Big(\frac{y-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)/3}\Big)\left[A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(\eta_{j,\delta}ib^{\prime\prime}f\big)(y)+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(y)}{b(y)-\lambda-i\alpha}\Big[\varphi\left(\frac{y-y_{j\ast}}{\delta_{0}}\right)-\varphi\left(\frac{y-y_{j\ast}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\right)\Big]f(y)\right]

\displaystyle=\varphi\Big(\frac{y-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)/3}\Big)A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big(\eta_{j,\delta}ib^{\prime\prime}f\big)(y).

(7.8)

Finally, we can write

A_{\Theta}^{-1}\big[(1-\eta_{j,\delta})ib^{\prime\prime}f\big]=A_{\Theta}^{-1}\left[1-\varphi\Big(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta_{0}}\Big)ib^{\prime\prime}f\right]+A_{\Theta}^{-1}\left[\varphi\Big(\frac{\cdot-y_{j*}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\Big)ib^{\prime\prime}f\right].

(7.9)

Combining (
7.1
), (
7.1
), and (
7.9
) concludes the proof.
∎

In order to prove the limiting absorption principle for
(\alpha,\lambda)\in R_{dj}
we will need to prove bounds for
T_{\Theta}
on
X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
for
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
given by (
4.3
) as well as their properties in higher regularity spaces. We summarize all the required results in the following propositions.

Proposition 7.1
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}},\,j\in\{1,2\}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1]
,
\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
satisfying
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-2,1)
we have the following bounds on
T_{\Theta}
for
f\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
,

\displaystyle\norm{T_{\Theta} f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\langle k\delta(\Lambda)\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}},

(7.10)

and for
k\delta\lesssim 1
and
|y-y_{j\ast}|\gtrsim\delta_{0}
,

\big|T_{\Theta}f(y)\big|\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\varrho_{j}^{-\sigma_{1}-1/4}\varrho_{j,k}^{-\sigma_{2}+1/4}(y;\Lambda)\,\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(7.11)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\norm{\tc{v}{1}f }_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}+\norm{\tc{v}{2} f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\epsilon^{1/2}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{2}}\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(7.12)

In particular, in the definition (
1.38
),
C^{\dagger}
is chosen so that we have the bound

\displaystyle\norm{\tc{v}{1} f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}+\norm{\tc{v}{2} f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(7.13)

In order to prove the requisite compactness needed for the proof of the limiting absorption principle, we will need a more detailed understanding of the regularity of
T_{\Theta}
than those implied by Proposition
7.1
. The type of regularity we can prove for
T_{\Theta}
will depend on whether
\lambda
is in the “intermediate regime”:
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll|b(y_{j*})-\lambda|\ll 1
, or the “viscous regime”:
|\lambda-b(y_{j*})|\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

We firstly consider the intermediate region.

Proposition 7.2
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}},\,j\in\{1,2\}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
, and
k\delta\leq N
, for some
N\geq 1
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
,
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll|\lambda-b(y_{j*})|\ll 1
,
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1]
,
\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
satisfying
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-2,1)
we have the following bounds for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

•

(bounds on
T_{I1}
)

\displaystyle\big|\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}f(y)\big|\lesssim_{N}\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\varrho_{j,k}^{2}(y;\Lambda)k^{\sigma_{2}},\,\beta\in\{0,1,2\};

(7.14)

•

(bounds on
T_{I2}
)
We can write
\partial_{y}T_{I2}f
as

\displaystyle\partial_{y}T_{I2}f

\displaystyle=\Upsilon_{I2}+R_{I2},

(7.15)

where
\Upsilon_{I2}:\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\mathbb{C}
is compactly supported in
\{y:\,|y-y_{j\ast}|\leq\delta(\Lambda)\}
,
R_{I2}:\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\mathbb{C}
, and satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\Big[(|\xi|+1)|\mathcal{F}(\Upsilon_{I2})(\xi)|\Big]\lesssim_{N}\frac{\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{1+\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}},

(7.16)

\displaystyle\big|T_{I2}f(y)\big|+\varrho_{j,k}(y;\Lambda)\big|R_{I2}(y)\big|+\varrho_{j,k}^{2}(y;\Lambda)\big|\partial_{y}R_{I2}(y)+d_{j,k}^{-1}\Upsilon_{I2}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\frac{\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}}\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho_{j}(y;\Lambda)};

(7.17)

•

(bounds on
T_{v1}
)

\displaystyle\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)|\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{v1}f(y)|\lesssim_{N}\frac{\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\delta(\Lambda)\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\varrho_{j}(y;\Lambda)\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}},\quad\beta\in\{0,1,2\},

(7.18)

where
c_{1}>0
is sufficiently small (and more precisely is given by Lemma
8.1
below);

•

(bounds on
T_{v2}
)

\displaystyle|\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{v2}f(y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\epsilon\delta(\Lambda)\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{4}\varrho_{j}(y;\Lambda)\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}},\quad\beta\in\{0,1\}.

(7.19)

We now turn to the viscous region.

Proposition 7.3
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}},\,j\in\{1,2\}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
, and
k\delta\leq N
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
,
|\lambda-b(y_{j*})|\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and
f\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
satisfying
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1]
,
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-2,1)
the following bounds hold for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

(bounds on
T_{I1}
)

\displaystyle\big|\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}f(y)\big|\lesssim_{N}\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\varrho_{j,k}^{2}(y;\Lambda)k^{\sigma_{2}},\,\beta\in\{0,1,2\};

(7.20)

•

(bounds on
T_{I2}
)

\displaystyle\big|\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I2}f(y)\big|+\big|\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{v1}f(y)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}}\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho_{j}(y;\Lambda)},\,\beta\in\{0,1,2\};

(7.21)

•

(bounds on
T_{v1}
and
T_{v2}
)

\displaystyle\big|\varrho_{j,k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{v2}f(y)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\epsilon\delta(\Lambda)\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{4}\varrho_{j}(y;\Lambda)\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}},\quad\beta\in\{0,1\},

(7.22)

\displaystyle\big|\varrho_{j,k}^{2}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{2}T_{v2}f(y)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\varrho_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{j,k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)}.

(7.23)

Remark 7.2
.

The implicit constants appearing in Propositions
7.1
,
7.2
and
7.3
may grow with
N
. However, in the application of these propositions in the proof of the limiting absorption principle, we will only need to consider the case where
N
is fixed.
We will only prove Propositions
7.2
and
7.3
under the assumption that
N=1
, but an inspection of the proofs shows the general case follows with perhaps a larger constant.

Remark 7.3
.

The role of higher regularity is to capture the “uniform compactness” of
T_{\Theta}
(in the sense given in Lemma
6.4
). Some care is needed here as the function space
X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
itself has non-trivial
\lambda
dependence. The essential feature is that, upon rescaling by
\delta(\Lambda)
, the higher order regularity estimates encode compactness on the fixed space
H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. This is made precise in section
7.2.2
.

The proofs of Propositions
7.1
,
7.2
, and
7.3
are very important but are somewhat technical. The full proofs will be given in Section
8
.

7.2.
The compactness argument

Using Propositions
7.1
,
7.2
, and
7.3
we are now ready to prove the limiting absorption in the degenerate case. For the ease of notations, we assume that
y_{j\ast}=0
and also drop the dependence of various quantities on
j\in\{1,2\}
which is fixed, when there is no possibility of confusion.

We firstly consider the intermediate region where the spectral parameter is
|\nu/k|^{1/2}
away from the critical value.

Proposition 7.4
.

Fix
\gamma\in[7/4,2)
.
Assume that

(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in\{(0,-\gamma),(1,-\gamma+1),(1-2(2-\gamma),-\gamma+2)\},

(7.24)

and let
\sigma_{0}
be as in Proposition
3.1
.
There exists
\epsilon_{0,I}\in(0,1/8),\kappa_{I}>0,M>0
, depending on
\gamma
, such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0,I})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
\alpha\in[-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1]
, and
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\setminus[-M\epsilon^{1/2},M\epsilon^{1/2}]
with
j\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\norm{f + T_{\Theta}f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\geq\kappa_{I}\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}

(7.25)

for all
f\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
.

We now turn to the viscous region where
|\lambda|\lesssim|\nu/k|^{1/2}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\gamma,(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}),\text{ and }M
be as in Proposition
7.4
.
There exist
\epsilon_{0,v}\in(0,1/8)
,
\kappa_{v}>0
, and
\sigma_{0}(M)\in(0,1)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0,v})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
\alpha\in[-\sigma_{0}(M)\epsilon^{1/2},1]
, and
\lambda\in[-M\epsilon^{1/2},M\epsilon^{1/2}]
we have

\norm{f + T_{\Theta}f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\geq\kappa_{v}\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}

(7.26)

for all
f\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
.

Remark 7.4
.

In order to prove Proposition
7.5
we will need to choose
\sigma_{0}(M)
small based on
M
(see (
7.65
)). However, the proof of Proposition
7.4
only requires smallness of
\sigma_{0}
in a way such that Proposition
3.1
holds.

If we set
\kappa:=\min(\kappa_{I},\kappa_{v})
,
\epsilon_{0}=\min(\epsilon_{0,I},\epsilon_{0,v})
, and
\sigma_{\sharp}:=\min(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{0}(M))
then Propositions
7.4
and
7.5
immediately imply the following result.

Proposition 7.6
.

Fix
\gamma\in[7/4,2)
.
Assume that

(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in\{(0,-\gamma),(1,-\gamma+1),(1-2(2-\gamma)),-\gamma+2)\}.

(7.27)

Then there exists
\epsilon_{0},\kappa,\sigma_{\sharp}>0
, depending on
\gamma
, such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0,})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
,
\alpha\in[-\sigma_{\sharp}\epsilon^{1/2},1]
, and
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
with
j\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\norm{f + T_{\Theta}f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\geq\kappa\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}

(7.28)

for all
f\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
.

In the rest of the section we give the proof of Proposition
7.4
and Proposition
7.5
, beginning with the proof of Proposition
7.4
.

Proof of Proposition
7.4
.

Suppose (
7.25
) does not hold. Then there exist

(f_{\ell},\kappa_{I,\ell},\epsilon_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell},k_{\ell},\alpha_{\ell},M_{\ell}),\quad{\rm with}\,\,(\kappa_{I,\ell},\epsilon_{\ell},M_{\ell}^{-1})\to 0+,\lambda_{\ell}\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}\setminus\big[-M_{\ell}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2},M_{\ell}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2}\big],

and
\norm{f_\ell}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}=1
, such that

\norm{f_\ell+ T_{\Theta_\ell} f_\ell}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\leq\kappa_{I,\ell}.

(7.29)

In (
7.29
) we have introduced
\Theta_{\ell}
to represent dependence on
\epsilon_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell},k_{\ell},\alpha_{\ell}
.

We claim that we may assume
(\alpha_{\ell},M_{\ell}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2},\lambda_{\ell})\to 0
. To see this, we first note that

\liminf_{\ell\to\infty}{M_{\ell}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2}}<\infty,

otherwise there is nothing to prove by our assumption on the range of the spectral parameter
\lambda_{\ell}
. We may therefore assume, passing to subsequences if necessary, that
(\alpha_{\ell},M_{\ell}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2},\lambda_{\ell})
converges.
If
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\alpha_{\ell}
or
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\lambda_{\ell}
are not zero, then
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})>0
.
In this case we may follow similar arguments as in the proof Proposition
6.1
, using the higher order regularity proved for
T_{I1}
,
T_{I2}
in Propositions
7.2
and
7.3
, and the bounds proved for
T_{v1}
and
T_{v2}
on
X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
in Proposition
7.1
, which ensure the needed convergence for
T_{\Theta_{\ell}}f_{\ell}
.

We can therefore assume that
(\alpha_{\ell},\epsilon_{\ell},M_{\ell}\epsilon_{\ell}^{1/2},\lambda_{\ell})
converges to 0, which implies that
\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})
converges to 0. Proposition
7.1
also implies that there exists a subsequence such that

k_{\ell}\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})\lesssim 1.

There are two main cases to consider: in the first case, a uniform-in-
\ell
amount of
f_{\ell}
stays
\delta_{0}
distance away from the critical point (the “Non-concentrated” case); in the second case, the sequence
f_{\ell}
concentrates near the critical point (the “Concentrated” case). More precisely, we distinguish two scenarios:

(1)

The Non-Concentrated case

\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\big\|\varrho^{-\sigma_{1}}\varrho_{k_{\ell}}^{-\sigma_{2}}(\cdot;\Lambda)f_{\ell}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\backslash S_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}})}>0,

(7.30)

(2)

Concentrated case

\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\big\|\varrho^{-\sigma_{1}}\varrho_{k_{\ell}}^{-\sigma_{2}}(\cdot;\Lambda)f_{\ell}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\backslash S_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}})}=0.

(7.31)

7.2.1.
The non-concentrated case

It follows from (
7.11
), (
7.29
) and (
7.30
) that
|k_{\ell}|\lesssim 1
.
We introduce the notation

\displaystyle T_{I1\ell}f(y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k_{\ell}}(y,z;\Lambda_{\ell})A_{\Theta_{\ell}}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}f)\,dz,

(7.32a)

\displaystyle T_{I2\ell}f(y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k_{\ell}}(y,z;\Lambda_{\ell})A_{\Theta_{\ell}}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})}b^{\prime\prime}f)\,dz,

(7.32b)

\displaystyle T_{v1\ell}f(y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k_{\ell}}(y,z;\Lambda_{\ell})\varphi_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})}{{3}}}(z)A_{\Theta_{\ell}}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})}b^{\prime\prime}f)\,dz,

(7.32c)

\displaystyle T_{v2\ell}f(y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k_{\ell}}(y,z;\Lambda_{\ell})(1-\varphi_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})}{{3}}}(z))\frac{\epsilon\partial_{z}^{2}A_{\Theta_{\ell}}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})}b^{\prime\prime}f)}{b(z)-\lambda_{\ell}-i\alpha_{\ell}}\,dz.

(7.32d)

By Propositions
7.2
and
7.3
,
it follows that for all
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
with
k_{\ell}\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})\lesssim 1
and
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we have

\displaystyle|T_{I2}f(y)|+|T_{v1}f(y)|+|T_{v2}f(y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})\norm{f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\varrho(y;\Lambda_{\ell})\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}},

(7.33)

where we used the fact that
\epsilon_{\ell}\lesssim\delta^{4}(\Lambda_{\ell})
.

The pointwise inequality (
7.33
) directly implies that

\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\left(\norm{\tc{I}{2 \ell}f_\ell}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}+\norm{\tc{v}{1\ell}f_\ell}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}+\norm{\tc{v}{2\ell}f_\ell}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\right)=0,

(7.34)

for
p>1
with
(\gamma-1)p<1
.

We now show that we can extract a convergent sequence whose limit satisfies Rayleigh’s equation. By weak compactness of
L^{p}
, we can find
f_{*}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
such that

f_{\ell}\rightharpoonup f_{*}
. This weak convergence can be upgraded to strong convergence using (
7.29
), (
7.34
), and the higher regularity of
T_{I1\ell}
.
Passing
\ell\to\infty
, we obtain that

f_{*}+\lim_{\ell\to\infty}T_{I1\ell}f_{*}=0\quad\text{in }L^{p}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}).

(7.35)

Using Lemma
4.1
, we obtain that
f_{*}
is bounded and moreover, for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}

|f_{*}(y)|=|\lim_{\ell\to\infty}T_{I1\ell}f_{*}(y)|\lesssim y^{2}.

(7.36)

To compute
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}T_{I1\ell}f_{*}
, we observe that
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
and
A_{\ell}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}f_{*})
have convergent subsequences in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
from Arzela-Ascoli, Lemma
2.1
, and Lemma
6.5
. Therefore

\lim_{\ell\to\infty}T_{I1\ell}f_{*}=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{*}(y,z)A_{*}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}f_{*})\,dz

(7.37)

where we have defined
\mathcal{G}_{*}:=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
and
A_{*}^{-1}:=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}A_{\ell}^{-1}
. By the proof of Lemma
6.5
we have

A_{*}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}f_{*})=\frac{(1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}}(y))b^{\prime\prime}(y)f_{*}(y)}{b(y)}

(7.38)

Additionally, for
z\neq 0
,

\mathcal{L}_{*}\mathcal{G}_{*}(y,z):=\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}+k_{*}^{2}+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(y)}{b(y)}\varphi_{\delta_{0}}(y)\right)\mathcal{G}_{*}(y,z)=\delta(y-z)

(7.39)

in the sense of distributions.
Applying
\mathcal{L}_{*}
to (
7.35
) gives

\left(-\partial_{y}^{2}+k_{*}^{2}+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(y)}{b(y)}\right)f_{*}(y)=0,

(7.40)

for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, in the sense of distributions. This
contradicts our main spectral assumption
1.1
.

7.2.2.
The concentrated case

For the remainder of the proof we adopt the shorthand
\delta_{\ell}=\delta(\Lambda_{\ell})
with a slight abuse of notation when
\ell\in\{0,1,2\}
. Here our main concern is when
\ell\to\infty
so there should be no possibility of confusion.

By (
7.13
), (
7.29
), (
7.31
) and Lemma
4.1
, we can find an
N
such that for
\ell\geq N
we have

\norm{f_\ell+ T_{\Theta_\ell} f_{\ell}}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}+\norm{(\tc{v1}{\ell} + \tc{v}{2\ell})
f_\ell}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}+\norm{\tc{I}{1 \ell}f_\ell}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\leq\frac{2}{3},

(7.41)

which implies that

\norm{\tc{I}{2 \ell}f_\ell}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\geq\frac{1}{3}.

(7.42)

Since
T_{I2\ell}f_{\ell}=T_{I2\ell}(\varphi_{2\delta_{\ell}}f_{\ell})
and
T_{I2\ell}
are uniformly bounded on
X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
by Proposition
7.1
,
it follows from (
7.42
) that there exists
\theta>0
which is independent of
\ell
, such that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}\Big[\delta_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\norm{f_\ell}_{L^{2}(S_{\delta_{\ell}})}+\norm{\delta_\ell\p_yf_{\ell}}_{L^{2}(S_{\delta_{\ell}})}\Big)+\norm{ f_\ell}_{L^{\infty}(S_{4\delta_{\ell}}\setminus S_{\delta_{\ell}})}+\norm{ \delta_\ell\p_y f_\ell}_{L^{\infty}(S_{4\delta_{\ell}}\setminus S_{\delta_{\ell}})}\Big]\geq\theta.

(7.43)

In view of the fact that a non-vanishing amount of
f_{\ell}
concentrates near the critical point, we use a blow-up argument.
To this end, define the re-scaled quantities

\displaystyle(Y,Z)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{y}{\delta_{\ell}},\frac{z}{\delta_{\ell}}\right)\quad(y,z)\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2},

(7.44a)

\displaystyle B_{\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{-2}b(y),

(7.44b)

\displaystyle F_{\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}f_{\ell}(y),

(7.44c)

\displaystyle G_{\ell}(Y,Z)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{k_{\ell}}(y,z;\Lambda_{\ell}),

(7.44d)

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}(Q_{\ell})(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{2+\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}A_{\Theta_{\ell}}^{-1}(q)(y),\quad\text{ where }Q_{\ell}(Y):=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}q(y).

(7.44e)

It will be convenient to extend the rescaled functions so that they are defined on
\mathbb{R}
. For the
Y
values not in the range of
\frac{y}{\delta_{\ell}},y\in[-\mathfrak{p}/2,\mathfrak{p}/2]
, we simply extend all of the functions as zero. In this way, we can use all of the previously obtained bounds to control the sequence.

It follows from rescaling that
G_{\ell}
satisfies for
|Y|<\mathfrak{p}/(2\delta_{\ell})
and
|Z|<\mathfrak{p}/(2\delta_{\ell})
,

-\partial_{Y}^{2}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)+(k_{\ell}\delta_{\ell})^{2}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)+\frac{B_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(Y)(\varphi(\delta_{0}^{-1}\delta_{\ell}Y)-\varphi(Y))}{B_{\ell}(Y)-(\lambda_{\ell}+i\alpha_{\ell})\delta_{\ell}^{-2}}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)=\delta(Y-Z),

(7.45)

and that
\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}(Q_{\ell})(Y)
satisfies for
|Y|<\mathfrak{p}/(2\delta_{\ell})
and
|Z|<\mathfrak{p}/(2\delta_{\ell})
,

\frac{\epsilon_{\ell}}{\delta_{\ell}^{4}}\partial_{Y}^{2}\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}(Q_{\ell})(Y)-i\left(B_{\ell}(Y)-\frac{\lambda_{\ell}-i\alpha_{\ell}}{\delta_{\ell}^{2}}\right)\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}(Q_{\ell})(Y)=Q_{\ell}(Y).

(7.46)

Crucially, (
7.43
) and (
7.44
) give uniform-in-
\ell
lower bounds on
F_{\ell}
in
H^{1}[-1,1]
intersection with

W^{1,\infty}\big([-4,4]\setminus[-1,1]\big).

Finally, we define, with (
7.44a
),

\displaystyle K_{\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}T_{\Theta_{\ell}}f_{\ell}(y),

(7.47a)

\displaystyle K_{1\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}T_{I1\ell}f_{\ell}(y),

(7.47b)

\displaystyle K_{2\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}T_{I2\ell}f_{\ell}(y),

(7.47c)

\displaystyle K_{3\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}T_{v1\ell}f_{\ell}(y),

(7.47d)

\displaystyle K_{4\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle:=\delta_{\ell}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}T_{v2\ell}f_{\ell}(y).

(7.47e)

Using (
7.44
) and (
7.32
), we obtain that

\displaystyle K_{1\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}\left(\left(1-\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{\delta_{\ell}}}\right)B_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}F_{\ell}\right)(Z)\,dZ,

(7.48a)

\displaystyle K_{2\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}\left(\varphi B_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}F_{\ell}\right)(Z)\,dZ,

(7.48b)

\displaystyle K_{3\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)\varphi\left(\frac{Z}{3}\right)\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}\left(\Phi(\delta_{\ell}\cdot)B_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}F_{\ell}\right)(Z)\,dZ,

(7.48c)

\displaystyle K_{4\ell}(Y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{\ell}(Y,Z)\left(1-\varphi\left(\frac{Z}{3}\right)\right)\frac{\epsilon_{\ell}}{\delta_{\ell}^{4}}\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta}(\delta_{\ell}\cdot)B_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}F_{\ell})(Z)}{B_{\ell}(Z)-\lambda_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}^{-2}-i\alpha_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}^{-2}}\,dZ.

(7.48d)

Propositions
7.2
and
7.3
imply for
\beta\in\{0,1\}
and
1\leq|Y|\ll\delta_{\ell}^{-1}
that

\displaystyle\min\{1+|Y|,(k_{\ell}\delta_{\ell})^{-1}\}^{\beta}|\partial_{Y}^{\beta}K_{1\ell}(Y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{\ell}^{2-|\sigma_{2}|}(1+|Y|^{2}),

(7.49a)

\displaystyle\min\{1+|Y|,(k_{\ell}\delta_{\ell})^{-1}\}^{\beta}|\partial_{Y}^{\beta}K_{2\ell}(Y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|Y|},

(7.49b)

\displaystyle\min\{1+|Y|,(k_{\ell}\delta_{\ell})^{-1}\}^{\beta}|\partial_{Y}^{\beta}K_{3\ell}(Y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\exp\left(-c_{0}\delta_{\ell}^{2}\epsilon_{\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}{1+|Y|},

(7.49c)

\displaystyle\min\{1+|Y|,(k_{\ell}\delta_{\ell})^{-1}\}^{\beta}|\partial_{Y}^{\beta}K_{4\ell}(Y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{\ell}}{\delta_{\ell}^{4}(1+|Y|)}.

(7.49d)

From the higher order regularity estimates proved in Propositions
7.2
and
7.3

it follows that
K_{\ell}
is relatively compact in
H^{1}([-1,1])
and
W_{{loc}}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1])
.
We conclude that
K_{\ell}
has a convergent subsequence in
H_{\rm loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, the limit of which we call
K_{*}
. Combined with (
7.29
) and (
7.44
), this implies that there exists a subsequence such that
F_{\ell}
converges to
F_{*}
in
H_{\rm loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. From the convergence of
F_{\ell},K_{\ell}
and (
7.29
) we have that

F_{*}+K_{*}=0.

(7.50)

From the spatial decay of
K_{\ell}
in (
7.49
) we can deduce that
F_{*}
is in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.
To further understand the limiting solution
F_{*}
,
we observe that

\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{\epsilon_{\ell}}{\delta_{\ell}^{4}}\lesssim\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{1}{M_{\ell}^{2}}=0.

(7.51)

Therefore, from (
7.49
), for
Y,Z\in\mathbb{R}
,

K_{*}=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}K_{2\ell}=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{*}(Y,Z)\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}\left(\varphi b^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*}\right)\,dZ,

(7.52)

where
G_{*}
satisfies for
Y,Z\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}_{*}}G_{*}:=-\partial_{Y}^{2}G_{*}(Y,Z)+k_{*}^{2}G_{*}(Y,Z)+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)(1-\varphi(Y))}{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{2}Y^{2}-\lambda_{*}-i\alpha_{*}}G_{*}(Y,Z)=\delta(Y-Z),

(7.53)

with
\lambda_{*}:=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\lambda_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}^{-2}
,
\alpha_{*}:=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\alpha_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}^{-2}

and
k_{*}:=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}k_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}
(passing to subsequences if necessary to ensure that all sequences converge).
Given the definition of
\delta(\Lambda)
, one of the following must be true:

(1)

|\alpha_{*}|>0
,

(2)

|\lambda_{*}|>0
.

By using the re-scaled bounds for the relevant functions, we can follow the proof of Lemma
6.5
, and obtain that
\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}(Q)(Y)
has the following limiting behavior:

•

If
\alpha_{*}\neq 0
or if
\frac{\lambda_{*}}{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}<0
, then in the sense of distributions for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{\delta_{\ell}^{-4}\epsilon_{\ell}\to 0,\,\delta_{\ell}^{-2}\alpha_{\ell}\to\alpha_{*},\,\delta_{\ell}^{-2}\lambda_{\ell}\to\lambda_{*}}\widetilde{A}_{\ell}(Q)(Y)=\frac{-Q(Y)}{\alpha_{*}+i(\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)Y^{2}}{2}-\lambda_{*})},

(7.54)

•

If
\alpha_{*}=0
and
\frac{\lambda_{*}}{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}>0
, then in the sense of distributions for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta_{\ell}^{-4}\epsilon_{\ell}\to 0,\,\delta_{\ell}^{-2}\alpha_{\ell}\to 0,\,\delta_{\ell}^{-2}\lambda_{\ell}\to\lambda_{*}}\widetilde{A}_{\ell}(Q)(Y)=\text{P.V.}\frac{-Q(Y)}{i(\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)Y^{2}}{2}-\lambda_{*})}+\pi\sum_{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)c^{2}}{2}=\lambda_{*}}\frac{Q(c)}{|b^{\prime\prime}(0)c|}\delta(Y-c).

(7.55)

If
\alpha_{*}\neq 0
or if
\frac{\lambda_{*}}{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}<0
, then (
7.54
) implies that for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{*}(Y,Z)\widetilde{A}_{\ell}^{-1}\left(\varphi b^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*}\right)\,dZ=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{*}(Y,Z)\frac{\varphi(Z)b^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*}(Z)}{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)Z^{2}}{2}-\lambda_{*}-i\alpha_{*}}\,dZ.

(7.56)

Applying
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{*}
to (
7.50
)
we get that

-\partial_{Y}^{2}F_{*}+k_{*}^{2}F_{*}+\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{2}Y^{2}-\lambda_{*}-i\alpha_{*}}F_{*}=0,

(7.57)

for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
in the sense of distributions. This contradicts the well-known fact that the linearized operator associated with the Poiseuille flow has no discrete eigenvalues.

Now assume that
\alpha_{*}=0
and
\frac{\lambda_{*}}{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}>0
. Using (
7.55
)
we obtain that for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
,

F_{*}(Y)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{G_{*}(Y,Z)(1-\varphi(Z))b^{\prime\prime}(0)F(Z)}{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{2}Z^{2}-\lambda_{*}}dZ+i\pi\sum_{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)c^{2}}{2}=\lambda_{*}}\frac{G_{*}(Y,c)F_{*}(c)}{|b^{\prime\prime}(0)c|}=0,

(7.58)

which implies that for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
,

-\partial_{Y}^{2}F_{*}(Y)+k_{*}^{2}F_{*}(Y)+\text{P.V.}\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{2}Y^{2}-\lambda_{*}}F_{*}(Y)+i\pi\sum_{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)c^{2}}{2}=\lambda_{*}}\frac{F_{*}(c)}{|b^{\prime\prime}(0)c|}\delta(Y-c)=0,

(7.59)

in the sense of distributions. Multiplying
\overline{F_{\ast}}
with (
7.59
), integrating over
\mathbb{R}
and taking the imaginary part, we see that
F_{\ast}(c)=0
. This again contradicts with the fact that the linearized operator associated with the Poiseuille flow has no discrete eigenvalues. The proof is now complete.
∎

Now that we have quantified the boundary between the intermediate viscous regime through
M
, we can give the proof of Proposition
7.5
.

Proof of Proposition
7.5
.

The proof proceeds identically to that of Proposition
7.4
except in the concentrated case in section
7.2.2
. For
\lambda\in[-M\epsilon^{1/2},M\epsilon^{1/2}]
we no longer have (
7.51
).
Since
\frac{\epsilon_{\ell}}{\delta_{\ell}^{4}}
is bounded, it has a convergent subsequence, the limit of which we denote as
\epsilon_{*}
. If
\epsilon_{*}=0
, then we argue as in Proposition
7.4
. Therefore, we assume that
\epsilon_{*}>0
. From (
7.50
),

K_{*}
is now of the form

\displaystyle K_{*}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}(K_{2\ell}+K_{3\ell}+K_{4\ell})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{*}(Y,Z)\widetilde{A}_{*}^{-1}\left(\varphi b^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*}\right)dZ+\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{*}(Y,Z)\varphi\left(\frac{Z}{3}\right)\widetilde{A}_{*}^{-1}\left(b^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*}\right)dZ

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{*}(Y,Z)\left(1-\varphi\left(\frac{Z}{3}\right)\right)\epsilon_{*}\frac{\partial_{Z}^{2}\widetilde{A}_{*}^{-1}(b^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*})}{\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}{2}Z^{2}-\lambda_{*}-i\alpha_{*}}\,dZ.

(7.60)

Using the regularity of
K_{*}
, we can bootstrap the regularity of
F_{*}
to prove that
F_{*}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and satisfies for
Y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}\partial_{Y}^{2}W_{*}-\alpha_{*}W_{*}-i\left(\frac{b^{\prime\prime}(0)Y^{2}}{2}-\lambda_{*}\right)W_{*}+ib^{\prime\prime}(0)F_{*}=0,

\displaystyle W_{*}=(\partial_{Y}^{2}-|k_{*}|^{2})F_{*}.

(7.61)

Our goal is to derive a contradiction by showing the solution of (
7.2.2
) must be zero. To this end, we use a direct energy method.
Multiplying by
\overline{W_{*}}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{Y}W_{*}(Y)|^{2}dY+\alpha_{*}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|W_{*}(Y)|^{2}dY=0,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{1}{2}Y^{2}-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}\right)|W_{*}(Y)|^{2}dY+\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{Y}F_{*}(Y)|^{2}+|k_{*}F_{*}(Y)|^{2}dY=0.

(7.62)

If
\frac{\lambda_{*}}{b^{\prime\prime}(0)}\leq 0
we have a contradiction so we assume that it is positive. Similarly, if
\alpha_{*}\geq 0
we immediately have a contradiction so we also assume that
-\sigma_{0}(M)\epsilon_{*}^{1/2}\leq\alpha_{*}<0
. Combining (
7.2.2
) with the inequality

\norm{f}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\leq 2\norm{Yf}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\norm{\p_Yf}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})},

(7.63)

we obtain that

\norm{W_*}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\leq 2\left(\frac{\lambda_{*}|\alpha_{*}|}{\epsilon_{*}b^{\prime\prime}(0)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\norm{W_*}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

(7.64)

This is a contradiction because

\left|\frac{\lambda_{*}|\alpha_{*}|}{\epsilon_{*}b^{\prime\prime}(0)}\right|\leq\frac{M\sigma_{0}(M)}{|b^{\prime\prime}(0)|},

(7.65)

and we can choose
\sigma_{0}(M)>0
sufficiently small so that
M\sigma_{0}(M)<\frac{|b^{\prime\prime}(0)|}{4}
.

∎

8.
Proof of Proposition
7.1
- Proposition
7.3

In the section we provide detailed proofs for Proposition
7.1
- Proposition
7.3
. Recall that
\epsilon\in(0,1/8),j\in\{1,2\}
,
(\alpha,\lambda)\in R_{dj}
(see (
7.1
) for the definition of
R_{dj}
), and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Since
j
and
y_{j\ast}
are fixed, we often drop the dependence of various quantities on
j
and assume that
y_{j\ast}=0
, when there is no possibility of confusion. We also allow the implied constants to depend on the indices
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
.

Before proving the bounds on
T_{\Theta}
, we first prove the following technical lemma which will be useful for quantifying the heuristic that
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)
is exponentially localized to the support of
f
. Recall the definition (
3.35
) for
d_{j,k}
, which we denote as
d_{k}
below.

Lemma 8.1
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{1/2},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Furthermore, suppose that
c\in[2,4]
, and that
D\subset\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an interval. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
f\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
with
\text{supp}(f)\subset D
,

\sigma_{1}\geq 0,\,\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R},
the following bounds hold.

•

For

y\in S_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{c}},D\subset\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
, we have

\big|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)\big|\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\norm{ f}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\frac{\exp\big(-c_1\delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)}{d_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}\delta(\Lambda)^{2+\sigma_{1}}};

(8.1)

•

For
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus S_{c\delta(\Lambda)},D\subset S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
, we have

\begin{split}|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)|&\lesssim\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}\epsilon^{-1/2}\exp(-c_1|y|\delta(\Lambda)\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\end{split}

(8.2)

where
c_{1}>0
is a small constant independent of
(\epsilon,\alpha,k,\lambda)
.

Proof.

We first prove (
8.1
).
For
z\in\text{supp}(f)
and
y\in S_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{c}}
we have
|y-z|=|y-\text{sgn}(y)\delta(\Lambda)|+|\text{sgn}(y)\delta(\Lambda)-z|
. Then, we can write

\exp(-L(y,z)|y - z|)=\exp(-L(y,z)\left|y - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)\times\exp(-L(y,z)\left|z - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)

(8.3)

where
L(y,z)
represents the coefficient of
|y-z|
in the exponential in (
3.4
) and (
3.3
). For
z\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
and for
y_{0}
satisfying
b(y_{0})=\lambda
,
|y_{0}|\leq\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{c}

we have
|b(z)-\lambda|\gtrsim|b(z)|
and
L(y,z)\gtrsim\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
, with an implicit constants only depending on
b
. This implies there exists
c_{1}
, depending only on
b
such that

\displaystyle\exp(-L(y,z)\left|y - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\quad y\in S_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{c}},

\displaystyle\exp(-L(y,z)\left|z - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}} \left|z - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|),\quad z\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus S_{\delta}(\Lambda),

(8.4)

and consequently,

\displaystyle|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)|\lesssim\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\int|f(z)|\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)^{-1}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}} \left|z - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)\,dz,

\displaystyle\quad y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}.

(8.5)

The bound (
8.1
) will follow from (
8
) if

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(z;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(z;\Lambda)}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}} \left|z - \text{sgn}(y) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)\,dz\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}}d_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}}.

(8.6)

To prove (
8.6
) we consider several cases. First, assume that
k\delta\geq 1
. Then
\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(z;\Lambda)=\frac{1}{k^{\sigma_{2}}}
. Therefore, it is enough to only consider
\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(z,\Lambda)
. Using that
y\in S_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{c}}
and
\varrho(z,\Lambda)\geq\delta(\Lambda),
(
8.6
) follows. Similar considerations hold for
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
and
\sigma_{2}\geq 0
. Now, consider the case where
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
and
\sigma_{2}<0
. In this situation

\frac{1}{\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y,\Lambda)}\lesssim\frac{\varrho^{|\sigma_{2}|}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)}\lesssim\frac{\varrho^{|\sigma_{2}|}(y;\Lambda)}{\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}}}.

(8.7)

Therefore, it’s sufficient to prove that

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{|\sigma_{2}|}(z;\Lambda)\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}} \left|z - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)\,dz\lesssim\delta(\Lambda)^{|\sigma_{2}|}.

(8.8)

Since
c_{1}\delta(\Lambda)\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})
is an approximation of the identity and
\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta(\Lambda)}\leq\delta(\Lambda)
, (
8.8
) follows, completing the proof of (
8.1
).

For the proof of (
8.2
) we proceed as before except with the roles of
y
and
z
reversed from (
8
). This yields

\displaystyle\exp(-L(y,z)\left|y - \text{sgn}(y) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) |y |\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\quad y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus S_{c\delta(\Lambda)},

\displaystyle\exp(-L(y,z)\left|z - \text{sgn}(y) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}} \left|z - \text{sgn}(y ) \delta(\Lambda) \right|),\quad z\in S_{\delta(\Lambda)}.

(8.9)

Then, for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus S_{c\delta(\Lambda)}

\displaystyle|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y)|\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) |y |\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}\epsilon^{-\frac{2}{3}}\min(\delta(\Lambda),\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}})^{-\frac{1}{3}}

(8.10)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda) \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}} \left|z - \text{sgn}(y) \delta(\Lambda) \right|)\,dz,

(8.11)

which implies the desired bounds.
∎

We will prove of Propositions
7.1
-
7.3
by proving the needed bounds for each
T_{I1},T_{I2},T_{v1}
, and
T_{v2}
individually. Each of the following subsections is dedicated to one of the operators and proves the bounds needed for the proof of the propositions.

8.1.
Bounds on
T_{I1}

Recall that the definition of
T_{I1}
with
y_{1*}=0
is

T_{I1}g(y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz,\quad y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}.

(8.12)

Since the
(1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g
is supported approximately
\delta_{0}
away from the critical layer
we can essentially view
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
as a bounded multiplication operator. However, in order to avoid the logarithmic loss in
\delta(\Lambda)
for
\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)
applied to bounded functions, we use Lemma
8.1
to view
A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)
as if it was supported
\delta_{0}
distance away from the origin. The following Lemma implies Propositions
7.2

and
7.3
for
T_{I1}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
with
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
we have the following bounds for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\beta\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\big|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}g(y)\big|\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\varrho_{k}^{2}(y;\Lambda)k^{\sigma_{2}}\times\begin{cases}k^{-1},&{\rm if}\,\,\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\varrho(y,\Lambda),\\
1,&{\rm if}\,\,\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\frac{1}{k}.\end{cases}

(8.13)

Proof.

First, suppose that
\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\varrho(y;\Lambda)
.
Decompose
T_{I1}g
as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{3}}(z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz

(8.14)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)(1-\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{3}}(z))A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz.

Proposition
3.1
and (
8.1
) with
\delta=\delta_{0}
imply

\displaystyle|(1-\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{3}}(z))A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}k^{\sigma_{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})},

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{3}}(z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\exp(-c_1 \delta_0^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})k^{\sigma_{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(8.15)

Lemma
2.1
combined with (
8.1
) for
\beta\in\{0,1\}
yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\varrho^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{3}}(z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\varrho^{2}(y;\Lambda)\exp(-c_1 \delta_0^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{k^{\sigma_{2}}\varrho_{k}(z,\Lambda)}{\varrho^{2}(z,\Lambda)}\,dz

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\varrho^{2}(y;\Lambda)k^{\sigma_{2}-1}\delta(\Lambda)^{-1}\exp(-c_1 \delta_0^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

(8.16)

Similarly, for
\beta\in\{0,1\}
we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\varrho^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)(1-\varphi_{\frac{\delta_{0}}{3}}(z))A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz\right|\lesssim_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\frac{\varrho^{2}(y;\Lambda)}{k^{1-\sigma_{2}}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(8.17)

Since
\delta(\Lambda)\gtrsim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
it follows

\delta(\Lambda)^{-1}\exp(-c_1 \delta_0^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\lesssim 1,

(8.18)

giving (
8.13
) for
\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\varrho(y;\Lambda)
and
\beta\in\{0,1\}
.
For
\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\frac{1}{k}
, we bound
A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)
as

|A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)|\lesssim_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}k^{\sigma_{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(8.19)

Combining (
8.19
) with Lemma
2.1
then yields (
8.13
) for
\beta\in\{0,1\}
.
For
\beta=2
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\partial_{y}^{2}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=-A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(y)+(V(y)+k^{2})\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}((1-\varphi_{\delta_{0}})b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz.

(8.20)

By (
8.19
),
the rest of the proof proceeds as for
\beta=0
.
∎

The following corollary implies Proposition
7.1
for
T_{I1}
.

Corollary 8.1
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1]
,
\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
, we have the following bounds for
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
,

\norm{\tc{I}{1}g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(8.21)

Proof.

Recalling the definition of
X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
, it is sufficient to prove that for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\beta\in\{0,1\}
we have

\displaystyle\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}g(y)|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(8.22)

Since
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is bounded and
\sigma_{1}\geq 0
it is enough to prove

\displaystyle\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}g(y)|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(8.23)

We first consider the case
\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\varrho(y;\Lambda)
. From (
8.13
) it follows that

\displaystyle\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)|\varrho^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}g(y)|\lesssim\varrho^{\sigma_{1}+2}(y;\Lambda)k^{\sigma_{2}-1}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(8.24)

where we used that
\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
. Now assume that
\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)=\frac{1}{k}
; (
8.13
) yields

\displaystyle k^{-\sigma_{2}}|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I1}g(y)|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k^{2}},

(8.25)

which completes the proof.
∎

8.2.
Bounds on
T_{I2}

Recall that the definition of
T_{I2}
with
y_{1*}=0
is given for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
by

T_{I2}g(y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz.

(8.26)

Unlike the other terms in the decomposition of
T_{\Theta}
, the kernel
of
T_{I2}
exhibits singular behavior due to the critical layer. This requires a more careful analysis near the critical layer than the pointwise estimates given by the fundamental solution bounds (see Lemma
8.5
). However, the Airy fundamental solution bounds are still useful, even for
|y|\lesssim\delta(\Lambda)
,
provided
that we have the bound

|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(y)|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta})}}{\varrho^{2}(y;\Lambda)},

(8.27)

with the implicit constant independent of
\alpha,\epsilon,k,\lambda
. Comparing this with the bounds in Proposition
3.1
, we determine that (
8.27
) will happen in the following three situations:

\displaystyle\delta(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\frac{1}{2}},

(8.28a)

\displaystyle\delta(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}},

(8.28b)

\displaystyle\delta(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\lesssim|b(y)-\lambda|^{1/2}\,\,{\rm for}\,\,y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}},\quad{\rm and}\,\,\lambda\notin b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}),

(8.28c)

with implicit constants independent of
\epsilon,\alpha,k,\lambda
. We refer to the situations in (
8.28
) as
regularly degenerate
. In these situations, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 8.3
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Furthermore, assume that at least one of the cases in
(
8.28
)
holds. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
,
g\in L^{\infty}(S_{\delta})
with
{\rm supp}\,g\subseteq S_{\delta}
, and all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
the following bounds hold,

•

If
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
, then we have

\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)|\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I2}g(y)|\lesssim\frac{\delta(\Lambda)\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\left[\frac{\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\right]^{1/4},\quad\beta\in\{0,1,2\};

(8.29)

•

If
k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle|T_{I2}g(y)|+\Big|\frac{1}{k}\partial_{y}T_{I2}g(y)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta})}}{k^{2}\varrho^{2}(y;\Lambda)}.

(8.30)

Proof.

We provide the details only for (
8.29
) since the proof of (
8.30
) is simpler. We decompose
T_{I2}
as

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{3\delta(\Lambda)}(z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz+\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)(1-\varphi_{3\delta(\Lambda)}(z))A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz.

(8.31)

By Lemma
2.1
it follows that for
\beta\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\Big|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\partial_{y}^{\beta}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)(1-\varphi_{3\delta(\Lambda)}(z))A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\delta\wedge\frac{1}{k}\big)\min\Big\{\frac{\delta}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)},\,e^{-k\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\Big\}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1-\varphi_{3\delta(\Lambda)}(z))|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)|\,dz.

(8.32)

On the support of
(1-\varphi_{3\delta(\Lambda)}(z))
, (
8.2
) implies

\begin{split}&|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta}(\Lambda)b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)|\\
&\lesssim\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}\epsilon^{-1/2}\exp(-\frac{1}{2}c_1|z |\delta(\Lambda)\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\exp(-\frac{c_1}{2} \frac{\delta(\Lambda)^2}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}),\end{split}

(8.33)

where
c_{1}
is as in Lemma
8.1
. Since

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1-\varphi_{3\delta(\Lambda)}(z))\delta(\Lambda)\,\epsilon^{-1/2}\exp(-\frac{c_1}{2}|z|\delta(\Lambda)\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\,dz\lesssim 1,

(8.34)

The desired bound (
8.29
) for
\beta\in\{0,1\}
follows from (
8.33
)-(
8.34
) and (
8.2
).

For the second derivatives, (
2.7
) yields

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda))}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=-A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(y)+(V(y)+k^{2})\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz.

(8.35)

The desired bound (
8.29
) for
\beta=2
then follows from (
8.2
) and (
8.33
)-(
8.34
).
For the proof of (
8.30
), we do not decompose
T_{I2}
: We directly use (
8.27
), Lemma
2.1
, and that
ke^{-k|z|}
is an approximation of the identity.
∎

Due to Lemma
8.3
, we can assume the following conditions hold for the remaining bounds on
T_{I2}
:

\displaystyle|\lambda|\gg\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\text{ with an implicit constant independent of }\epsilon,\alpha,k,\lambda,

(8.36a)

\displaystyle\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}),

(8.36b)

\displaystyle\delta(\Lambda)\approx\delta_{1}(\lambda),

(8.36c)

where we recall that
\delta_{1}(\lambda)=8\sqrt{|\lambda|/|b^{\prime\prime}(0)|}
.
We seek to isolate the part of
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)
that is supported at a distance closer to the critical layer than the length scale
d_{k}
of the modified Green’s function. On the complement of this region, the smallness from the Green’s function can balance the size of
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)
exactly as in the “regularly degenerate” situation covered in Lemma
8.3
.

Following (
6.5
),
we decompose
T_{I2}
as

\displaystyle T_{I2}g

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{2}T_{I2\ell}g.

(8.37)

We define
T_{I20}
and
T_{I2\ell},\ell\in\{1,2\}
as

\displaystyle T_{I20}g(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\Big(1-\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}^{2}(z-y_{\ell})\Big)\,dz,

(8.38)

and

\displaystyle T_{I2\ell}g(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}^{2}(z-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz,

(8.39)

where
\ell\in\{1,2\}
,
b(y_{\ell})=\lambda

and
\theta\in(0,1)
is a constant only depending on
b(y)
(see Lemma
8.5
).
Since
\delta(\Lambda)=\delta_{1}(\lambda)
, for
\theta
sufficiently small,
\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{1})
and
\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{2})
have disjoint supports. Using a similar proof as for Lemma
8.3
, we obtain the following bounds for
T_{I20}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Assume that,
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
, and let
\theta
to be fixed in the range given by Lemma
8.5
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
g\in L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})

we have the following bounds for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

•

If
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
, then

\displaystyle\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)|\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I20}g(y)|\lesssim\frac{\delta(\Lambda)\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\left[\frac{\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\right]^{1/4},\quad{\rm for}\,\,\beta\in\{0,1,2\},

(8.40)

•

If
k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{k^{\beta}}\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I20}g(y)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}}{k\varrho(y;\Lambda)},\quad{\rm for}\,\,\beta\in\{0,1\}.

(8.41)

Proof.

We only consider the case where
k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1
since the case for
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
is identical to Lemma
8.3
.
We have the following:

\displaystyle|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)(1-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}^{2}(z-y_{\ell}))|\lesssim\begin{cases}\frac{k\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta}(\Lambda))}}{\delta(\Lambda)}&|z|\leq\delta(\Lambda)\\
\frac{\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta}(\Lambda))}}{\varrho^{2}(z,\Lambda)}&|z|\geq\delta(\Lambda)\end{cases}

(8.42)

Combining these estimates yields

\Big|A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)(1-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}^{2}(z-y_{\ell}))\Big|\lesssim\frac{k\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta}(\Lambda))}}{\varrho(z,\Lambda)}.

(8.43)

Therefore, by Lemma
2.1
we have

\Big|\frac{1}{k^{\beta}}\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I20}g(y)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}}{k\varrho(y;\Lambda)},\quad{\rm for}\,\,\beta\in\{0,1\},

(8.44)

which proves the result.
∎

For the region that lies in the support of
\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})
with
b(y_{\ell})=\lambda
, the size of
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)
is too large to be controlled by the pointwise bounds for the modified Green’s function directly. As in the invisicd case we expect that integrating by parts to “remove” a derivative from
A_{\Theta}^{-1}
and placing it on
\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)
would be beneficial to gain from the larger scale of variation of
\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)
(see
[
38
]
,
[
39
]
). The following lemma makes this precise using a frequency based characterization of the inverse of the generalized Airy operator.

Lemma 8.5
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Furthermore, assume that
\delta(\Lambda)\approx\delta_{1}(\lambda)
,
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, and
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll|\lambda|\ll 1
with implicit constants independent of
(\epsilon,\alpha,k,\lambda)
. Then there exists
\theta_{0}\in(0,1/2)
, depending only on
b(y)
, such that for all
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0}]
and
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
the following statement holds.
Suppose that
f:\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfies

\norm{f}_{L^{2}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}+d_{k}\norm{\p_y f}_{L^{2}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}\leq M,

(8.45)

and is 0 outside of
S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
. We view the function

\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)(y),

(8.46)

as a function on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then

\big|\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)\right)(\xi)\big|\lesssim\frac{M}{d_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)},

(8.47)

and

\norm{ (1 + d_k \p_y)^{-1} \left(\varphi_{ \theta d_k} (\cdot- y_{\ell})\ai(f) \right)}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\frac{M}{d_{k}\delta(\Lambda)},

(8.48)

where
y_{\ell}
satisfies
b(y_{\ell})=\lambda
.

The following inequality will be useful in the proof of Lemma
8.5
:

\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}(I)}\lesssim|I|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\norm{f}_{L^{2}(I)}+|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}\norm{\p_y f }_{L^{2}(I)}

(8.49)

where
I
is an interval and
|\cdot|
denotes the length of the interval.

Proof.

Assuming (
8.47
), by Plancherel’s theorem we have

\displaystyle\norm{ (1 + d_k \p_y)^{-1} \left(\varphi_{\theta d_k}(\cdot- y_{\ell})\ai( f) \right)}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\norm{ (1 - id_k \xi)^{-1} \mathcal{F}(\varphi_{\theta d_k}(\cdot- y_{\ell}) \ai(f))(\xi) }_{L_{\xi}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{M}{d_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)}\norm{(1 - id_k \xi)^{-1}}_{L_{\xi}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\frac{M}{d_{k}\delta(\Lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Therefore, it suffices to prove
(
8.47
)
. We proceed as in Lemma
6.3
. For non-critical values of
\lambda
,
b^{-1}(\lambda)
has cardinality 2:
b^{-1}(\lambda)=\{y_{1},y_{2}\}
. Define
\varphi_{\ell}(y):=\varphi\left(\frac{2(y-y_{\ell})}{\theta d_{k}}\right)
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. Choose
\theta_{0}>0
so that the supports of
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
are separated by distance
\theta_{0}d_{k}
. Furthermore, define
w_{c,\ell}
as the solution to the equation for
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\epsilon\partial_{y}^{2}w_{c,\ell}(y)-\alpha w_{c,\ell}(y)+ib^{\prime}(y_{\ell})(y_{\ell}-y)w_{c,\ell}(y)=f(y_{\ell}).

(8.50)

We can solve for
w_{c,\ell}
explicitly as

w_{c,\ell}(y,y_{\ell})=\frac{\ f(y_{\ell})}{b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})}\Big(\frac{b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}W\Big(\Big(\frac{b^{\prime}(y_{\ell})}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}(y-y_{\ell})\Big),\quad y\in\mathbb{R},

(8.51)

where
W
is given in the Fourier variable
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
as

\widehat{W}(\xi)=-\sqrt{2\pi}\exp\left(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}+\frac{\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}\alpha\xi}{(b^{\prime}(y_{\ell}))^{\frac{2}{3}}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,0]}(\xi).

(8.52)

We define
w_{R}:\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\mathbb{C}
as

\displaystyle w_{R}:=A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f)-\sum_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\varphi_{\ell}w_{c,\ell},

(8.53)

where we have view
\varphi_{\ell}w_{c,\ell}
as a function on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
w_{R}
can be bounded similarly as in Lemma
3.4
(see (
3.57
)), and is more regular than
W
.
Therefore, we obtain from (
8.52
) that

\big|\mathcal{F}(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(f))(\xi)\big|\lesssim\frac{M}{d_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)}\quad j\in\{1,2\}

(8.54)

which completes the proof.
∎

For the remainder of the section on
T_{I2}
we will take
\theta
to be fixed. Before proceeding with the proof of the bounds for
T_{I2j}
, it will be convenient to introduce the following notation.
For
\ell\in\{1,2\}
define for
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\Xi_{\ell}(y):=(1+d_{k}\partial_{y})^{-1}\left[\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(\cdot-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)\right].

(8.55)

where
b(y_{\ell})=\lambda
and
\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})
is as in Lemma
8.5
. We now proceed with obtaining the remaining bounds for
T_{I2\ell}
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Assume that
\ell\in\{1,2\}
,
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Furthermore, assume that
\delta(\Lambda)=\delta_{1}(\lambda)
,
\lambda\in b(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, and
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll|\lambda|\ll 1
with implicit constants independent of
\epsilon,\alpha,k,\lambda
. Take
\theta
to be fixed in the range given by Lemma
8.5

Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
g\in L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})
the following bounds hold for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

If
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
, then

\begin{split}&|T_{I2\ell}g(y)|+\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)\big|\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}g(y)+d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\big|\\
&\lesssim\left[\frac{\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\right]^{1/4}\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})},\\
&\varrho_{k}^{2}(y,\Lambda)\big|\partial_{y}\big(\partial_{y}T_{I2j}g(y)+d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\big)+\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\big|\\
&\lesssim\left[\frac{\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\right]^{1/4}\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}.\end{split}

(8.56)

•

If
k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle|T_{I2\ell}g(y)|+\frac{1}{k}\big|\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}g(y)+d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\exp\big(- k\max(|y| - \delta_1(\lambda), 0) \big)}{k\delta(\Lambda)}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})}.

(8.57)

Proof.

First, observe that
T_{I2\ell}
can be written as

\displaystyle T_{I2\ell}g(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}^{2}(z-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1-d_{k}\partial_{z})\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\right)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz.

(8.58)

Similarly,
\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}
can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}g(y)=\partial_{y}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}^{2}(z-y_{\ell})A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\varphi_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1-d_{k}\partial_{z})\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\right)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz,

\displaystyle=d_{k}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\partial_{y}^{2}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle-d_{k}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\partial_{y}(\partial_{y}+\partial_{z})\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\right)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\partial_{y}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\right)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz.

(8.59)

The equation for
\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)
implies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\partial_{y}^{2}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=-\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)+(V(y)+k^{2})\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\right)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz.

(8.60)

To summarize, we write

\displaystyle\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}g(y)=-d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)+\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}R_{\ell}(y,z)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz,

(8.61)

where
\text{supp}(R_{\ell}(y,\cdot))\subset\left[y_{\ell}-\theta d_{k},y_{\ell}-\theta d_{k}\right]
, and

\displaystyle|R_{\ell}(y,z)|\lesssim\begin{cases}e^{-k|y-z|},\quad&d_{k}=\frac{1}{k},\\
\min\Big\{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)},e^{-k|y-z|}\Big\},&d_{k}=\delta(\Lambda).\end{cases}

(8.62)

Therefore,

\displaystyle|T_{I2\ell}g(y)|+\varrho_{k}(y)\left|\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}g(y)+d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\frac{\exp{- k\max\big(|y| - \delta_1(\lambda), 0\big) }}{k\delta(\Lambda)}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})},&{\rm if}\,\,d_{k}=\frac{1}{k},\\
\left[\frac{\varrho_{k}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\right]^{1/4}\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(S_{\delta(\Lambda)})},&{\rm if}\,\,d_{k}=\delta(\Lambda),\end{cases}

(8.63)

as desired.
To derive (
8.56
), observe that (
8.2
) and (
8.2
) imply

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\big(\partial_{y}T_{I2\ell}g+d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\big)

\displaystyle=-\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(y-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)+(V(y)+k^{2})\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+d_{k}\partial_{y}\Big(\big(V(y)+k^{2}\big)\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz\Big)

(8.64)

\displaystyle\quad-d_{k}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\partial_{y}^{2}(\partial_{y}+\partial_{z})\left(\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(z-y_{\ell})\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\right)\Xi_{\ell}(z)\,dz,

(8.65)

from which (
8.56
) follows.
∎

Combining Lemmas
8.3
,
8.4
, and
8.6
we get the following bounds for
T_{I2}
on
X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
.

Lemma 8.7
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{0}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
,
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1]
, and
\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
satisfying
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\leq 1
, we have the following bounds for
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
,

\norm{\tc{I}{2}g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\langle k\delta(\Lambda)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(8.66)

and for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
|y|\gtrsim\delta_{0}
,

\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)\big|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I2}g(y)\big|\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\varrho^{\sigma_{1}-1/4}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}+1/4}(y;\Lambda)\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}.

(8.67)

Proof.

We focus on (
8.66
), since (
8.67
) is easier as
|y|\gtrsim\delta_{0}
. We first assume that we are in the regularly degenerate case so that one of the cases in (
8.28
) holds. From Lemma
8.3
we have for
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)|\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{I2}g(y)|\lesssim\begin{cases}\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)\frac{\delta(\Lambda)\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}\varrho(y;\Lambda)},&{\rm if}\,\,k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1,\\
\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\frac{(k\delta(\Lambda))^{\frac{1}{2}}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k^{2}\varrho^{2}(y;\Lambda)\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}}},&{\rm if}\,\,k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1.\end{cases}

(8.68)

Using the fact that
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\leq 1
when
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
and that
\sigma_{1}\leq 1
when
k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1
implies the desired bound.
Now consider the singularly degenerate case (
8.36
).
For
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
we have from Lemma that

8.5

\delta(\Lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}d_{k}^{\sigma_{2}+1}\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}}\norm{d_k \varphi_{\theta d_k} (\cdot- y_{\ell}) \Xi_{\ell} }_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})},&{\rm if}\,\,k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1,\\
\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{k\delta(\Lambda)},&{\rm if}\,\,k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1,\end{cases}

(8.69)

where
d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(\cdot-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}
is as in Lemma
8.6
. Finally, (
•
‣
8.6
) implies that for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)\big|T_{I2j}g(y)\big|+\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{1+\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)\big|\partial_{y}T_{I2j}g(y)+d_{k}\varphi_{\theta d_{k}}(\cdot-y_{\ell})\Xi_{\ell}(y)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)\frac{\delta(\Lambda)\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}\varrho(y;\Lambda)},&\quad{\rm if}\,\,k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1,\\
\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{(k\delta(\Lambda))^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}}}\times\exp{- k\max(|y| - \delta_1(\lambda), 0) },&\quad{\rm if}\,\,k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1.\end{cases}

(8.70)

The fact that
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\leq 1
when
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
implies the desired bound.
∎

8.3.
Bounds on
T_{v1}

Recall the definition of
T_{v1}
(assuming that
y_{1*}=0
) for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

T_{v1}g(y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)\varphi_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{3}}(z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\,dz.

(8.71)

Heuristically, we expect that
T_{v1}
to be very small since for functions
g
that are supported at distances greater than
\delta(\Lambda)
away from the critical layer,
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(g)(y)
is exponentially small for
|y|<\delta(\Lambda)
from Lemma
8.1
. Crucially, Lemma
8.1
also has the correct scaling between
\delta(\Lambda)
and
\epsilon
: when
\delta(\Lambda)\approx\epsilon^{1/4}
, the exponential term is no longer small enough to view
T_{v1}
as negligible and we need higher regularity estimates for
T_{v1}
in order to prove the required compactness for the limiting absorption principle.

Lemma 8.8
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})

with
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1],\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
satisfying
\,\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-2,1)
, the following bounds hold for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

•

For
\beta\in\{0,1,2\}\text{ and }k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)|\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{v1}g(y)|\lesssim\frac{\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\delta(\Lambda)\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}};

(8.72)

•

For
\beta\in\{0,1\}
, we also have

\displaystyle\varrho_{k}^{\beta}(y;\Lambda)|\partial_{y}^{\beta}T_{v1}g(y)|\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\varrho^{-\sigma_{1}-1/4}\varrho_{k}^{-\sigma_{2}+1/4}(y;\Lambda)\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})},&k\delta(\Lambda)\lesssim 1,\\
k^{\sigma_{2}}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\varrho^{-\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\big(k\delta(\Lambda)\big)^{-2}\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})},&k\delta(\Lambda)\gtrsim 1,\end{cases}

(8.73)

where
c_{1}
is as in (
8.1
).

Corollary 8.2
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)

with
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1]
,
\sigma_{2}\in[-2,1/2]
satisfying
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-2,1)
the following bounds hold for
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})
,

\displaystyle\norm{\tc{v1}{}g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\begin{cases}\frac{\epsilon\norm{ g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\delta(\Lambda)^{4}}&k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1\\
\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{\langle k\delta(\Lambda)\rangle^{2}}&k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1,\end{cases}

(8.74)

where the implicit constants are is uniform in
(\alpha,\epsilon,k,\lambda)
.

Proof.

From (
8.1
) we have for
z\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\Big|\varphi_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{3}}(z)A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)\Big|\lesssim_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\exp(-c_1 \delta(\Lambda)^2 \epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}})\frac{\norm{g}_{X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})}}{d_{k}^{\sigma_{1}}\delta(\Lambda)^{2+\sigma_{2}}}.

(8.75)

The proof of (
8.72
) then follows from Lemma
2.1
. For (
8.73
), observe that since
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}<1
and
k\delta(\Lambda)\leq 1
it follows for
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
that

\frac{\delta(\Lambda)\varrho^{\sigma_{1}}(y;\Lambda)\varrho_{k}^{\sigma_{2}}(y;\Lambda)}{\varrho(y;\Lambda)\delta(\Lambda)^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}}\lesssim 1.

(8.76)

For the case of
k\delta(\Lambda)\geq 1
, we use that
ke^{-k|z|}
is an approximation of the identity.
The proof of corollary
8.2
is immediate.
∎

8.4.
Bounds on
T_{v2}

Recall from (
7.3
) the definition of
T_{v2}
assuming that
y_{1*}=0
,

T_{v2}g(y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{G}_{k}(y,z;\Lambda)(1-\varphi_{\frac{\delta(\Lambda)}{{3}}}(z))\frac{\epsilon\partial_{z}^{2}A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)(z)}{b(z)-\lambda-i\alpha}\,dz.

(8.77)

For
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll|\lambda|
, we expect that
T_{v2}
to be small since
A_{\Theta}^{-1}(\eta_{\delta(\Lambda)}b^{\prime\prime}g)
acts like multiplication by
\frac{1}{|b(y)-\lambda|+\delta(\Lambda)^{2}}
. However, some care is needed in the case when
|\lambda|\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
in order to ensure the correct behavior for
|y|\gg\delta(\Lambda)
(see (
8.79
)). Unlike
T_{v1}
, the kernel of
T_{v2}
is not localized to
S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
which complicates matters since it is no longer sufficient to simply consider the size of the kernel on
S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
. Capturing the optimal decay in the kernel away from
S_{\delta(\Lambda)}
is the reason why we integrate by parts in (
8.4
) and (
8.82
).

Lemma 8.9
.

Assume that
(\alpha,\lambda)\in(-\sigma_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},1)\times\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Then for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
and
g\in X^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(\mathfrak{M})

with
\sigma_{1}\in[0,1],\,\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}\in(-2,1)
the following bounds hold for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

•

For all values of
k\delta(\Lambda)
and all
\lambda\in\Sigma_{j,\delta_{0}}
we have
